- `d0c90f4` ARDETAIL.P2 (second of the AR-ACCOUNT-DETAIL chain): Reporting/Billing — the **dunning timeline display + account dunning figures** on `/billing/accounts/{account}` (the REAL state machine, READ-ONLY; fees tie). Per `docs/wireframe-parity/AR-ACCOUNT-DETAIL-DIFF.md` punch-list item #2 (now **RESOLVED**). **The timeline/stage is the REAL persisted state machine (max(DunningEvent.level) + the append-only dunning_events — NOT an "if age>N" page label); read-only (no dunning action this gate — send-reminder/escalation are later gates); the fee total ties to the real captured fee charges.** **Opening:** top commit `e750ad4` (ARDETAIL.P1), CI green; DemoClinicSeeder + a real overdue account run through the REAL DunningService. **STUDY (the crux — where the fee lives):** `dunning_events` stores ONLY `{level, triggered_on, status, document_path}` — **NO fee column.** A dunning fee is a SEPARATE captured `Charge` (code = the level's policy `fee_code`, `service_date` = the event's `triggered_on`, `line_total_minor` = the fee), captured by `DunningService::captureFee`→`ChargeCaptureService`; the only event↔charge link is the audit context. The `billing.dunning` SETTINGS policy defines the level thresholds + fee_codes (the demo configures `fee_code => 'DUNNING-FEE'`). The current stage = `max(DunningEvent.level)` (the P7/audit source). So: events give level/date/status (real); the fee must be sourced from the real captured charges (NOT fabricated per event). **(1) The method** `MetricsService::accountDunning($actor, string $accountId, array $feeCodeByLevel, $asOf): array` (billing.view, tenant-scoped): reads the account's dunning_events (across the account's invoices) ordered by date/level → `events[]` `{invoice_id, invoice_number, level, triggered_on, status, fee_minor}`; `current_stage` = max(level); `reminder_count` = count; per-event `fee_minor` = the REAL captured fee Charge matched by (account, `$feeCodeByLevel[level]`, service_date == triggered_on) — 0 when the level has no configured fee; `fees_minor` = Σ those; `fees_tie` = `fees_minor` === Σ ALL the account's captured dunning-fee charges (the recorded fees — a non-tie would surface a fee charge with no matching event, i.e. drift). The `$feeCodeByLevel` map (level⇒fee_code) is derived by the CONTROLLER from the `billing.dunning` policy (`AccountDetailController::dunningFeeCodeByLevel` via SettingsService + `DunningService::SETTINGS_KEY`) — so MetricsService stays free of settings/policy coupling. **(2) The page** `Billing/AccountDetail.vue` adds a **dunning timeline** (a vertical timeline of the real events: level/date/status + the real fee) + a **stage pill** (the real max-level stage) + the account dunning figures (stage · reminders sent · Σ fees) — READ-ONLY, **NO dunning action button** (send-reminder + Betreibung escalation are deferred, carefully-gated). i18n additive `billing.accountDetail.dunning.*`. **FENCE:** the timeline + stage are the REAL persisted state machine (displayed, NOT page-computed — no client "if age>N"); read-only (append-only events, no mutation, no action route added this gate); the fee total ties to the real charges (δ=0). No fabricated dunning step/fee (the timeline surfaces only persisted events; the fee is the real captured Charge). **NOTHING weakened:** the DunningService state machine / P1 ledger / the ReconciliationEngine / the reconcile invariant intact; MetricsService gained a read-only method; the P1 AccountDetail render test still passes (the new `dunning` prop is additive). **NO existing behavior test modified.** Added `tests/Feature/Billing/AccountDunningTest.php` (5 tests / 41 assertions, run through the REAL `DunningService::evaluate` — a 61-day-overdue invoice + a policy with level-1 fee `DUN-FEE` 1500 + level-2 no-fee): the timeline reads the real events (levels [1,2], both on the run date, === the persisted rows) with per-event fee (level 1 = 1500, level 2 = 0); the stage === `max(DunningEvent.level)` (=2, the real machine); the fee total 1500 === Σ the real captured DUN-FEE charges (fees_tie true); the page renders the engine timeline (assertInertia — dunning.* === the service) AND **adds no mutation route** (asserted: every `billing/accounts` route is GET-only); an account with no dunning run shows an honest empty timeline (stage 0, fees 0, tie true); billing.view (reception 403) + tenant-scoped (cross-tenant 404). **BROWSER-VERIFIED** (Playwright, org_admin Andrea/2FA, a real dunning run): Roland Brunner's account shows the dunning timeline with the real events (level 1 + level 2) + the stage pill + fees, read-only (no action button). VERIFIED locally: `npm run build` green (`AccountDetail-*.js` 6.6→9.7 kB); `composer check` FULLY green — Pint `passed`, PHPStan L5 `[OK]`, **Pest 1144 passed**; `composer test:smoke` green. composer check + CI verified after push. - `5d1fa85` ARDETAIL.P4 (fourth of the AR-ACCOUNT-DETAIL chain, and the page's FIRST consequential write): Billing — **record a payment through the guarded `PaymentService`** on `/billing/accounts/{account}` (over-allocation-guarded, reconciling δ=0, operator-gated, audited, append-only). Per `docs/wireframe-parity/AR-ACCOUNT-DETAIL-DIFF.md` punch-list item #6 (now **RESOLVED**). **Opening:** top commit `e9e69a6` (the docs/state reconciliation — a DOCS-ONLY child of `9c95246`/ARDETAIL.P3, so the gate's stated precondition holds: `git diff 9c95246..HEAD -- app Modules resources routes database tests config` is EMPTY), CI green (`check` → success); DemoClinicSeeder. **STUDY (the real path, not reimplemented):** `PaymentService::record()` appends a `Payment` (positive integer minor, method from `Payment::METHODS`, currency from the invoice else settings) and audits `payment.recorded`; `::allocate()` runs in ONE `DB::transaction` that locks the payment row + the `invoice_balances` row `FOR UPDATE`, refuses a non-issued/partially-paid invoice, then applies **THE GUARD** — `amountMinor > openBalance` → *"Cannot allocate more than the invoice open balance."*, `amountMinor > unallocated remainder` → *"Cannot allocate more than the payment unallocated remainder."* — appends the `PaymentAllocation`, refreshes the projection (`open = total − net allocations − net adjustments`; the PAID status stays payment-driven) and audits `payment.allocated`. Balances are DERIVED, never a stored counter. Permission = `billing.manage` (the service's own `authorize`). An overpayment is NOT rejected: it simply leaves a larger unallocated remainder (I3 allows it). **BUILT (a wire, not a payment path):** `POST /billing/accounts/{account}/payments` → `AccountDetailController@recordPayment` — validates shapes, resolves each chosen invoice tenant-scoped AND `where('patient_id', $patient->id)` (a forged foreign/other-account id 404s BEFORE any write), then calls `record()` + `allocate()` per target and surfaces the SERVICE's own refusal message via `withErrors(['record_payment' => …])`; on a refused line the receipt stands with an unallocated remainder (the `PaymentController::store` precedent — money received is never lost; a correction is a reversal row). `show` gained a `payment` prop (`can_record` reflect-only · `store_url` · `methods` · `open_invoices` = a SELECTION of the P1 engine ledger rows, never a recomputation); `AccountDetail.vue` got the record-payment card (amount/method/date/reference + a per-open-invoice allocation input prefilled from the engine's open balance, editable) using the existing `toMinor` input-normalisation idiom + a unique `billing.accountDetail.record.*` i18n block. **THE FENCE (proven):** the controller and the Vue contain NO money math and NO direct `Payment`/`PaymentAllocation`/`InvoiceBalance` write (adversarial grep clean — the only movements are `$payments->record` / `$payments->allocate`); no client sum/reduce. **OPERATOR-GATED + AGENT-EXCLUDED:** `billing.manage` only (reception 403 at the gate, before the service is reached); NO registered `AiTool` is a payment capability (the only financial tools are the advisory `billing.suggest_charge_codes`/`billing.preflight_invoice`, both `autonomyCeiling: APPROVE`), and neither `Modules/AiCore/src` nor `app/AiCore` references `PaymentService`/`PaymentAllocation`/the write route — the agent never commits money (D-151). **TESTS (8 ADDED, none modified):** `tests/Feature/Billing/AccountRecordPaymentTest.php` — posts through PaymentService (allocation + projection + `payment.recorded`/`payment.allocated` audit + append-only `LogicException` on update); THE GUARD (a forged over-allocation refused, 0 allocations, balance untouched, books still tie) and the remainder guard across two invoices; RECONCILE (I1–I6 δ=0 after the payment, and the P1 ledger / P7 rollup / `outstandingBalanceMinor` all move by exactly the payment and still tie); operator-gated (reception 403, nothing written); no agent path; tenant + account scoping; the page prop. Plus the FIX.5 route smoke extended with the new write route (reception → 403). **BROWSER-VERIFIED** (org_admin, Erika Baumgartner): CHF 100.00 recorded + allocated → balance CHF 313.00 → **CHF 213.00**, INV-1 issued → partially paid (paid CHF 100.00), running balance + "Ledger ties — CHF 213.00", overdue rollup 313 → 213; a forged CHF 999.99 allocation against a CHF 213.00 open balance refused in-page with *"Cannot allocate more than the invoice open balance."* and the balance UNCHANGED (the CHF 999.99 receipt standing fully unallocated); reconcile `check('2026-07')` and `check('2026-08')` both passed with all six deltas 0; reception GET **403** and a CSRF-valid POST **403**, nothing written. `npm run build` green; PHPStan L5 `[OK]`; Pint clean. See [[Billing]] / [[D-152]].

Per `docs/wireframe-parity/AR-ACCOUNT-DETAIL-DIFF.md` punch-list item #8 (now **RESOLVED**). **Opening:** top commit `5d1fa85` (ARDETAIL.P4), CI green (`check` → success); DemoClinicSeeder. **STUDY:** payment-plan/installment was genuinely WIREFRAME-NEW (grep over `Modules app database resources routes tests` empty). The outstanding source is the P1 population (series INV + frozen statuses, Σ the reconciled `invoice_balances.open_balance_minor`); the payment path is P4's `PaymentService::record` + `::allocate` (over-allocation-guarded, append-only, projection-refreshing, audited). **BUILT (additive):** migrations `payment_plans` + `payment_plan_installments` (integer minor; mutable rows so every recorded moment is `dateTime()` per P0P.G15; CHECK constraints total>0 / count>0 / amount>0); models `PaymentPlan` (active/completed/cancelled/defaulted) + `PaymentPlanInstallment` (pending/paid, `overdue` DERIVED from due-date vs. now, never a stored status that could drift); `PaymentPlanService` (`billing.manage` on every method). **THE TIE (no phantom money):** `create()` refuses a total above `accountOutstandingMinor()` — the SAME population the P1 ledger sums, so plan and ledger agree by construction — AND refuses a second active plan (so two plans can never together exceed the balance), and refuses a plan on a zero-balance account. **THE PARTITION:** the split is ENGINE-side (`intdiv($total,$n)` with the LAST installment absorbing the remainder — the only such line, `PaymentPlanService.php:333`), re-asserted against the persisted rows before commit, so a schedule that did not sum to its total could never reach the DB. **THE PLAN MOVES NO MONEY:** `payInstallment()` records through `PaymentService::record` then allocates oldest-first, each allocation CAPPED by that invoice's own open balance (respecting the guard rather than probing it); the leftover stays an honest unallocated remainder (I3); the installment then records WHICH payment settled it, and the plan auto-completes when nothing is pending. `cancel()`/`markDefaulted()` require a reason and are audited. **UI:** the payment-plan card on `Billing/AccountDetail.vue` (create form prefilled from the engine outstanding, schedule table, per-installment record button, cancel-with-reason) + a unique `billing.accountDetail.plan.*` i18n block; the Vue splits nothing and computes no money (adversarial grep clean; the controller is clean too). **AGENT-EXCLUDED:** no registered `AiTool` is a payment/installment/allocation capability (financial tools capped at APPROVE), and neither `Modules/AiCore/src` nor `app/AiCore` references `PaymentPlan`/`PaymentService`/the plan routes. **TESTS (11 ADDED):** `tests/Feature/Billing/PaymentPlanTest.php` — the exact partition (31300/3 → 10433+10433+10434, Σ===total===real outstanding); the plan total ties to the P1 ledger outstanding; over-the-balance + second-active-plan + zero-balance refusals; paying an installment records through PaymentService and the account reconciles I1–I6 δ=0 with the ledger/outstanding moving by exactly the installment; paying all completes the plan and settles the balance to 0; the over-allocation guard still binds when the balance is written down behind the plan (allocation capped, remainder honest); operator-gated (service throws + all three routes 403 for reception); no agent path; cancel/default reason + audit; tenant + account scoping; the page renders the engine plan. Route smoke extended with the plan-create write (reception → 403). **TWO EXISTING ASSERTIONS NARROWED AGAIN (flagged):** `AccountDetailLinksTest` + `AccountDunningTest` pin the page's exact write set — updated from the single P4 payments route to the four money/plan operator routes; the "no dunning/reminder/escalation route" invariant is untouched and still passes. **BROWSER-VERIFIED** (org_admin, Erika Baumgartner, CHF 313.00 outstanding): created a 3-installment plan → **CHF 104.33 + 104.33 + 104.34 = CHF 313.00** ("Schedule ties"), scheduling moved no money; recorded installments 1 and 2 → two REAL `bank_transfer` Payments of 10433 each, fully allocated, INV-1 issued → partially paid, balance CHF 313.00 → **CHF 104.34**, "Ledger ties — CHF 104.34"; cancelled the plan with a reason (audited); a CHF 500.00 plan against the CHF 104.34 balance was REFUSED in-page with *"A payment plan cannot schedule more than the account outstanding balance."* and nothing was written; reconcile `check('2026-07')` and `check('2026-08')` both passed with all six deltas 0 throughout. See [[Billing]] / [[D-153]].

Per `docs/wireframe-parity/AR-ACCOUNT-DETAIL-DIFF.md` punch-list item #7 (now **RESOLVED**). **Opening:** top commit `cb95de1` (ARDETAIL.P5), CI green (`check` → success); DemoClinicSeeder. **STUDY:** the dunning state machine is settings-driven (`billing.dunning` levels, ascending `days_past_due`, never skips a level, append-only `dunning_events`); its **TERMINAL stage = max(configured level)** (demo: level 2). Betreibung/escalation was genuinely wireframe-new. **BUILT (additive):** migration `debt_enforcement_escalations` — APPEND-ONLY (ORM guards + DB triggers, the `invoice_adjustments` recipe), `initiated_by` NOT NULL FK to `users` (so **no system/agent actor can ever be recorded as an initiator**), `confirmed_at`/`initiated_at` as `dateTime()` (P0P.G15), plus the eligibility evidence (`dunning_stage`, `outstanding_minor`) and the required `reason`; model `DebtEnforcementEscalation` (initiated | withdrawn, `supersedes_escalation_id`); `DebtEnforcementService`. **NEW PERMISSION `billing.escalate`** — deliberately **NARROWER than `billing.manage`**, which pharmacist/surgical-scheduler/ED/lab/radiology roles hold for charge capture: granted ONLY to `org_admin` + `billing`, so a clinical role that may capture a charge can NOT start a legal proceeding (a test proves the pharmacist holds billing.manage but is refused). **ELIGIBILITY (a real precondition, re-checked inside the service's own transaction):** the account must have reached the TERMINAL dunning level on ≥1 invoice AND still owe money AND have no live escalation; with NO dunning policy configured NOTHING is eligible (fail-closed — you cannot exhaust a process that does not exist). **OPERATOR-ONLY + EXPLICIT CONFIRMATION:** `initiate()` refuses without an explicit confirmation flag (validated `accepted` at the route, never defaulted) and without a reason; the confirmation moment is recorded. **AGENT-EXCLUDED BY CONSTRUCTION (the point of the gate):** no registered `AiTool` is an escalation capability (needles betreibung/escalat/enforce/debt/legal); no AiCore code references the service/model/permission/routes; **the ONLY files in `Modules/` + `app/` that reference `DebtEnforcementService`/`DebtEnforcementEscalation` are the service, the model and the operator-gated controller — asserted as an exact list**, so no job/command/listener/agent can reach it; `routes/console.php` schedules nothing enforcement-related; and the NOT-NULL user FK means every row names a person. "0 auto-escalated" is therefore structural, not a displayed number. **AUDITED + APPEND-ONLY:** initiate/withdraw both audit with `actor_type=user`; a withdrawal APPENDS a superseding row (the original is never mutated — proven), each escalation withdrawn at most once (unique index + lock). **UI:** the Betreibung card on `Billing/AccountDetail.vue` (eligibility evidence "reminder stage N of M", the operator-confirm checkbox + reason + optional official reference, the live escalation with who/when/why, withdraw-with-reason, full append-only history) + a unique `billing.accountDetail.enforcement.*` i18n block whose governance copy states only what the code enforces. **TESTS (8 ADDED):** `tests/Feature/Billing/DebtEnforcementTest.php` — eligibility only at the terminal stage (driven through the REAL `DunningService`, level 1 refused, level 2 allowed); fail-closed (no policy / nothing owed / already escalated); explicit confirmation + reason required, recorded, audited, append-only (update/delete throw); withdrawal appends and cannot be repeated; the dedicated permission (reception AND a billing.manage-holding pharmacist both refused, both routes 403 through the real stack); route-level confirmation validation + tenant/account scoping; **THE AGENT-EXCLUSION PROOF** (5 structural assertions); the page surfaces the real eligibility + escalation. Scheduling stays free of Clinical/Audit, arch tests green) — `appointment.manage` branch-scoped gate, string-id resolved in-controller (FIX.1 → cross-tenant/unknown id 404s); `resources/js/pages/Scheduling/AppointmentDetail.vue` (euca-tile-dark hero + glass-cards + timeline, the ARDETAIL.P3 idiom) + a unique `scheduling.appointmentDetail.*` i18n block; the day-board tile now carries `detail_url` and links to the page (`ScheduleGrid.vue`, optional prop so other callers are unaffected). **THE FENCE — everything real:** the status pill renders the TRUE status and labels **all EIGHT** machine states (the wireframe drew four); the source badge is the real `source`; the duration is the service's own; resources expose ONLY `{id,name,type}` — the wireframe's "scanner · X-ray" capability chips are **OMITTED, not hardcoded** (no backend field; deferred); the timeline shows real audit + reminder rows with the channel labelled exactly as recorded (**email — the page can never claim SMS**) and REAL provenance (a portal action is attributed "by the patient", a jobless row "recorded by the system" — never a fabricated "replied JA"); an honest empty state when no history exists. NO computed judgment (no no-show risk/priority/predicted duration), NO money, NO actions (action row = P2, reschedule = P3). **TESTS (7 ADDED):** `tests/Feature/Scheduling/AppointmentDetailTest.php` — renders the real appointment (status/source/duration/resources/patient/recorded allergy); **no fabricated backend** (resource keys are exactly `id,name,type`); the pill follows the REAL machine walked edge-by-edge (booked → confirmed → arrived → in_progress → completed) plus cancelled-with-reason and no_show; the timeline carries real audit rows with real from→to + actor and a reminder with its recorded `email` channel, asserting **no** 'sms'/'replied'/'whatsapp' string anywhere; an honest empty timeline; `appointment.manage` (billing role 403) + cross-tenant 404; the day-board `detail_url` drill link. Route smoke extended with the new detail route (a C-1-class surface). **BUG FOUND + FIXED IN-GATE:** vue-i18n resolves `.` as a path separator, so the audit-action labels (`appointment.booked`) never matched and the raw key rendered — the action keys are stored in underscore form and looked up via `replace(/\./g,'_')`, with an honest fallback to the raw key for an unmapped action. **BROWSER-VERIFIED** (org_admin): day-board tile → drill link → the page; a `booked` appointment shows **Booked**, a `confirmed` one **Confirmed**, a completed one **Completed** with the full walked history (Appointment booked → Confirmed [Booked → Confirmed] → Patient arrived → Treatment started → Completed, each "by Nadia Steiner"), a no-show showing its recorded reason; the real allergy chip (Pollen · mild); **no SMS / replied / scanner / X-ray / WhatsApp string on the page**; an unknown id 404s. See [[Scheduling]] / [[D-155]].
- `8874313` APPT.P2 (second of the APPOINTMENT-DETAIL chain): Scheduling/app-layer — the **action row driven by the REAL `LEGAL_TRANSITIONS`** on `/scheduling/appointments/{appointment}` (server-authoritative; the (a) true-status→legal-actions reconciliation; no illegal edge). Per `docs/wireframe-parity/APPOINTMENT-DETAIL-DIFF.md` punch-list item #2. **Opening:** top commit `54a4fde` (APPT.P1), CI green; DemoClinicSeeder. **STUDY (the real transition API):** `confirm/arrive/start/complete(Appointment,User)` · `noShow(...,?string $reason = null)` (reason OPTIONAL) · `cancel(...,string $reason)` (reason REQUIRED — the service itself throws on blank); `transition()` authorizes (branch-scoped `appointment.manage`), calls `assertLegal`, then re-asserts legality INSIDE a `lockForUpdate` transaction and dispatches `AppointmentTransitioned` → the app-layer audit listener writes `appointment.<to_status>` with `from_status`/`to_status`/reason. **`LEGAL_TRANSITIONS` is a `private const`.** **BUILT:** (1) a READ accessor `AppointmentService::legalTransitionsFrom(string $status): array` — additive, grants nothing, exposes the SAME map `assertLegal` enforces so the UI and the guard can never drift (the alternative, duplicating the map in the controller, is exactly the page-side allow the fence forbids); (2) `AppointmentDetailController::legalActions()` renders that set for the appointment's ACTUAL status, minus `rescheduled` (which needs the slot finder + overlap guard — APPT.P3); (3) `POST /scheduling/appointments/{appointment}/transition` → `AppointmentDetailController::transition`, which maps a verb to the REAL service method and decides NOTHING about legality; (4) the action row in `AppointmentDetail.vue` + a `scheduling.appointmentDetail.actions.*` i18n block. **THE (a) RECONCILIATION:** the page shows the TRUE status (P1), so it offers that status's legal moves — **booked → {Confirm, Cancel, No-show}** (arrive is NOT offered: `booked→arrived` is not an edge), **confirmed → {Mark arrived, Cancel, No-show}**, **arrived → {Start, Cancel}**, **in_progress → {Complete}**, **terminal → none**. No shortcut is composed here and no edge was added to the machine. **DELIBERATE CROSS-SURFACE DIVERGENCE (recorded):** the day-board's `DayBoardActionController` still composes `confirm()→arrive()` for a booked appointment (two legal steps, both audited — the audit's option (c)); this page instead reflects the machine literally (option (a)), per the gate's decision. Both are legal and neither weakens `LEGAL_TRANSITIONS`; they differ only in how many clicks reception needs. **REASON RULE (verified from the service, not invented):** cancel validates `required_if:action,cancel` because the service throws without one; **no_show's reason stays OPTIONAL** because the service permits null — the page does not invent a stricter server rule at one surface (P0D.GU: rules are enforced server-side, not page-side), and the test asserts exactly that. **TESTS (6 ADDED, 13 in the file):** the offered set equals `LEGAL_TRANSITIONS[status]` for booked/confirmed/arrived/in_progress and is EMPTY for completed + cancelled; THE RECONCILIATION (a booked appointment offers `confirm` and not `arrive`, asserted against `legalTransitionsFrom` itself, and offers `arrive` only once confirmed); an action moves through the real service and writes the real audit row (`actor_type=user`, actor = the operator, `from_status`→`to_status`); a FORGED illegal POST (`arrive`/`complete`/`start` from booked, and any move on a terminal appointment) is REFUSED with the machine untouched, and an unknown verb never reaches the service; cancel-without-reason refused + reason recorded verbatim, no-show reason optional-but-recorded; `appointment.manage` 403 + cross-tenant 404. **BROWSER-VERIFIED** (org_admin): booked showed **Confirm · Cancel appointment · Mark no-show** with **no "Mark arrived"**; confirmed showed **Mark arrived · Cancel · Mark no-show**; completed showed *"No further action is possible — this appointment is Completed."*; clicking Confirm moved it to **Confirmed**, updated the action set to the confirmed legal set and appended the real audit row (*Confirmed · Booked → Confirmed · by Andrea Lindenhof*); Cancel opened the required-reason field ("REASON FOR CANCEL APPOINTMENT"); and forged `complete`/`start` POSTs on a confirmed appointment were both **refused** ("Illegal appointment transition"), the status still **Confirmed**. **CI WENT RED ON THIS COMMIT AND THE FIX IS PART OF THE GATE: the real APPT.P2 tip is `27fa22c`, not `8874313`.** A test asserted the audit context by matching the RAW JSON substring `'"from_status":"booked"'`, which passes on dev MariaDB 10.4 and FAILS on CI MySQL 8 — MySQL 8 normalises a JSON column and re-serialises it with a space after the colon and the keys reordered (`{"to_status": "confirmed", "from_status": "booked"}`). Fixed by `json_decode`-ing the context and asserting the MEANING, never the serialised text; this is now the standing rule for every audit-context assertion (APPT.P3 follows it). **The lesson is the memory-worthy part: local-green is not CI-green, and a raw-JSON substring assertion is a latent cross-engine failure.** See [[Scheduling]] / [[D-156]].
- `ca90273` APPT.P3 (third of the APPOINTMENT-DETAIL chain — the page's CORE parity COMPLETE): Scheduling/app-layer — the **reschedule modal over the REAL `AvailableSlotFinder` + `reschedule()` overlap guard** (cannot double-book; availability re-checked at confirm). Per `docs/wireframe-parity/APPOINTMENT-DETAIL-DIFF.md` punch-list item #3. **Opening:** top commit `27fa22c` (the APPT.P2 CI-fix), CI green; DemoClinicSeeder. **STUDY (the real contract):** `AvailableSlotFinder::forServiceBranchDate(Service,$branchId,$date,$limit)` → conflict-free `{starts_at, ends_at, resource_ids}` (per-date by design; picks the first free resource of each required type — **no preferred-resource parameter**); `reschedule()` requires a reason (throws *"Reschedule requires a reason."*), `assertLegal(→ rescheduled)`, ONE `DB::transaction` with the old row `lockForUpdate`, frees its resource links, re-books via `BookingService::book` → `lockResource` → `assertNoOverlap` (throws `BookingConflictException::resourceTaken`), returns the NEW appointment and dispatches `AppointmentTransitioned` carrying `new_appointment_id`. **BUILT:** `reschedulePanel()` offers the modal ONLY when `rescheduled` is a legal move from the current status (reusing the P2 `legalTransitionsFrom`), and fills it by MERGING the finder's per-date answers across the next 14 days (≤4/day, ≤12 total, the appointment's own current slot excluded) — a merge of engine results, never a page-side availability computation; `POST /scheduling/appointments/{appointment}/reschedule` submits **only the chosen start time + reason** — the page never picks resources. **THE GUARD, TWICE OVER:** at confirm the controller RE-RUNS the finder for that date and requires the slot to still be conflict-free (taking ITS `resource_ids`), then `reschedule()` re-books behind `lockResource`→`assertNoOverlap`; so a slot taken between display and confirm is refused first by the re-check and, in any race that slips past, by the overlap guard itself. **A reschedule cannot double-book**, and *"availability re-checked server-side at confirm"* is literally true. On success the operator is redirected to the NEW appointment (the old one is terminal). **OMITTED, NOT FAKED:** the wireframe's "Dr. Weber only" toggle — the finder takes no preferred-resource parameter, so offering it would fabricate a filter the engine cannot honour (its own follow-on gate); browser-confirmed absent (0 checkboxes in the form). **TESTS (5 ADDED, 18 in the file):** the offered slots are each verified to appear in the REAL finder's answer for their date (and the current slot is never offered back); a terminal appointment offers no reschedule; a reschedule without a reason is refused with nothing re-booked; a valid move goes through `reschedule()` — old → `rescheduled` (terminal, resource links freed, reason recorded), a NEW appointment at the slot linked by `rescheduled_from_id` with the finder's 2 resources, both movements audited and the context DECODED (never a raw-JSON substring — the P2 MySQL-8 lesson) to confirm `from_status`/`to_status`/`new_appointment_id`; **THE GUARD** — a slot taken first by someone else is refused through the page path (appointment unmoved, no new row) AND calling `reschedule()` directly into it past the re-check throws `BookingConflictException`; `appointment.manage` 403 + cross-tenant 404. **BROWSER-VERIFIED** (org_admin): the modal listed real conflict-free finder slots with their resources (*08/17 08:00 · soonest · Anja Wyss · Sprechzimmer 1*, …) and the constraint chips; a no-reason POST was refused; confirming moved the appointment — the OLD one became **Rescheduled** (reason recorded, resources freed, "No further action is possible") and the operator landed on the NEW **Booked** appointment at 08/17 08:00; the practitioner toggle was absent. **A second reschedule into the same 08:00 time succeeded on DIFFERENT free resources (Dr. Brunner · Sprechzimmer 2) — correctly NOT a conflict; a DB check confirmed NO resource holds two overlapping blocking appointments, i.e. no double-book.** See [[Scheduling]] / [[D-157]].
- `4334017` AUTH-SEC.1 (first of the AUTH-SEC security sprint): Platform/app-layer — **remember-me no longer bypasses the 2FA challenge** (re-challenge on recall; 2FA stays mandatory). Fixes `docs/wireframe-parity/AUTH-SCREENS-DIFF.md` §4.1. **Opening:** top commit `e9b5783` (the auth audit), CI green. **THE DEFECT (reproduced in a browser by the audit):** after a full password+2FA login with "Remember me", deleting the session cookie and keeping ONLY the ~400-day `remember_web_*` cookie still opened `/app` with no password and **no 2FA challenge** — `EnsureTwoFactorEnabled` asserted the user had ENROLLED, never that THIS session had passed a challenge, so the recaller path was a standing second-factor bypass. **THE FIX:** the middleware now turns a recaller-restored session back into a PENDING two-factor login — signs the guard out (dropping the authenticated session AND the recaller), seeds `login.id`/`login.remember`, and redirects to the challenge; completing it re-issues a fresh recaller, so the PASSWORD factor stays remembered and the SECOND factor never is. **THE PROOF IS WRITTEN IN EXACTLY TWO PLACES,** both requiring a valid code in that session: the new app-layer `TwoFactorPassedResponse` (bound to Fortify's `TwoFactorLoginResponse`, so it runs only after a TOTP/recovery code validates) and a `TwoFactorAuthenticationConfirmed` listener (enrolment confirmation also requires a code — without it a freshly enrolled user would be bounced to a challenge). Being authenticated, enrolled or remembered never writes it. **TWO DELIBERATE SCOPING DECISIONS:** (1) the re-challenge is conditioned on the session having been restored FROM THE RECALLER, not "every session must carry the flag" — every other path already involved the second factor or is caught by the enrolment check, and a universal rule would reject `actingAs` sessions, where papering over it in the harness would have HIDDEN the very bypass being closed; (2) the check asks the WEB guard behind a `hasSession()` test, because `Auth::viaRemember()` proxies to the DEFAULT guard, which for Sanctum token requests is a `RequestGuard` with no such method — **a first attempt did exactly that and broke all 17 Nurse PWA API tests** (caught by `composer check`, which was RED before the fix; all 60 Nursing tests pass now). **NOTHING WEAKENED:** 2FA stays mandatory (no skip/disable), interactive login unchanged, enrolment enforcement/login throttle/suspended-tenant rejection untouched; `SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE=true` VERIFIED already present in `docs/DEPLOY-ENV.production.template:77` (not changed). **TESTS (7 ADDED):** `tests/Feature/Auth/RememberMeTwoFactorTest.php` — the bypass-closed proof (a via-remember session without a proof → the challenge, session left as a pending login), a proved session passes, an ordinary session untouched, the proof never written by merely authenticating, the real challenge records it and opens the app, interactive login still needs the second factor, un-enrolled still forced to enrol. The decision is asserted against the middleware because the HTTP test client cannot replay a recaller cookie through decryption (a harness limit, diagnosed before switching approach — not app behaviour). **BROWSER-VERIFIED with the audit's exact repro:** remember cookie alone now lands on `/two-factor-challenge`, completing it reaches the workspace and re-issues a fresh 400-day recaller. `composer check` FULLY green (Pest **1198 passed**/16029); smoke green. Commit `4334017`, **CI green**. See [[Platform]] / [[D-158]].
- `39c0413` AUTH-SEC.2 (second of the AUTH-SEC security sprint): app-layer/tests — **bound the password-reset views + added GUEST-ROUTE SMOKE** (a public 500 can't ship green again). Fixes `docs/wireframe-parity/AUTH-SCREENS-DIFF.md` §4.2/§7. **THE DEFECT:** Fortify's `resetPasswords()` was enabled so `/forgot-password` and `/reset-password/{token}` were REGISTERED, but no view was ever bound — both GET pages threw `BindingResolutionException` (**HTTP 500**), leaving a locked-out user with no self-service recovery. **THE SYMPTOM FIX:** `Fortify::requestPasswordResetLinkView()` + `Fortify::resetPasswordView()` bound to two new Inertia pages (`Auth/ForgotPassword.vue`, `Auth/ResetPassword.vue`, GuestLayout/Card/Input/Button idiom + a unique `auth.forgot.*`/`auth.reset.*` i18n block). NO auth rule changed: the POST flow, the signed-token check and the password policy (`ResetUserPassword` → `PasswordValidationRules`) are untouched, the response is identical for a known and an unknown address (no user enumeration), and a reset leaves mandatory 2FA intact (tested). **THE DISEASE FIX (the point):** every existing route smoke authenticates first, so NO PUBLIC page had ever been requested — which is exactly why a 500 on the pages an anonymous visitor meets shipped green. The FIX.5 smoke now drives the GUEST routes (`/login`, `/forgot-password`, `/reset-password/{token}`, `/invite/{token}`) with no `actingAs`. **PROVEN BY TEMPORARILY REMOVING THE NEW BINDINGS: the guest smoke then fails with exactly `guest.forgot-password -> 500` and `guest.reset-password -> 500`** (bindings restored, smoke green). **FLAGGED, NOT CHANGED:** the effective password policy is `Password::default()` (min 8 chars; no `Password::defaults()` configured — no mixed case/digit/symbol/breach check) — an explicit PRODUCT DECISION, not a fix to slip into a security sprint. **TESTS (5 ADDED + the guest smoke):** `tests/Feature/Auth/PasswordResetTest.php` — both pages render (they used to 500), the token/email carry through, the request answers identically for known/unknown addresses, the reset POST enforces the real policy (a short password refused, the old one still valid; a compliant one accepted), and a reset does not weaken mandatory 2FA. **BUG FOUND IN-GATE:** the first run was RED (`error code 255`, `Cannot redeclare prTenant()`) — Pest test files share a global namespace and my helpers collided with `BranchPractitionerResourceTest`; renamed to `pwrTenant`/`pwrUser` and swept every new helper/const for uniqueness. **BROWSER-VERIFIED:** both pages render **200** (was 500) and a real request returns *"We have emailed your password reset link."* See [[Platform]] / [[D-159]].
- `8a9a867` AUTH-VIS (**the FINAL gate of the nine-page WIREFRAME-PARITY PASS**): Platform/frontend — the 2FA enrolment **"Can't scan the code?" manual-secret fallback, rendering the user's OWN real provisioning secret**, plus the §3 state/copy polish. Closes `docs/wireframe-parity/AUTH-SCREENS-DIFF.md` §3.1/§3.2 + punch-list #4/#5. **Opening:** top commit `39c0413` (AUTH-SEC.2), tree clean, CI green (`check` → success). **STUDY:** `TwoFactorEnroll.vue` already fetched the QR + recovery codes over axios; Fortify's `GET /user/two-factor-secret-key` (route `two-factor.secret-key`, `TwoFactorSecretKeyController@show` → `Fortify::currentEncrypter()->decrypt($request->user()->two_factor_secret)`) already existed and was already in `EnsureTwoFactorEnabled`'s exemption list. **BUILT (purely display, no backend change):** a third parallel axios call for the secret; a reveal button beneath the QR; the secret rendered as `select-all font-mono` text; a `groupedSecret` computed that chunks it into four-character blocks joined with `·` — **display formatting only, never a new value**; three i18n keys under the existing `auth.twoFactor` block. **THE FENCE — REAL OR NOTHING:** the wireframe prints the fixed demo literal `JBSW·Y3DP·EHPK·3PXP`; that is **not** used and nothing is generated page-side. The value is server-provided and is the authenticated user's own key **by construction** — the endpoint takes no id, so there is no cross-user shape to guard against. **NO new exposure path:** the user's own secret, on their own enrolment screen, in the same auth context already rendering the QR that encodes the identical key, kept behind a reveal. **NOTHING WEAKENED:** 2FA stays mandatory-locked (SETTINGS.P4) — no skip/postpone/disable route added (asserted against the route table); AUTH-SEC.1's re-challenge + AUTH-SEC.2's reset bindings/guest smoke untouched and green. **ALSO IN-GATE (the §3.2 polish):** the enrolment card no longer renders **blank** while the QR/codes/secret are in flight (a real pending line that claims nothing about the outcome), and the recovery codes are marked `select-all`, matching the wireframe's "selectable text". The login card deliberately keeps **no** forgot-password link — the wireframe explicitly draws none (§2), so adding one would have been an invented affordance, not parity. **TESTS (5 ADDED):** `tests/Feature/Auth/TwoFactorSecretFallbackTest.php` — the endpoint returns the REAL decrypted secret and explicitly **not** the demo literal; the QR's provisioning URI carries the SAME secret the fallback shows (the two enrolment routes cannot drift); the answer is scoped to the authenticated user (two users, each gets only their own); a guest gets nothing (redirected to login); and 2FA stays mandatory with no skip/postpone/disable path in the route table. **BUG FOUND IN-GATE:** the guest assertion first failed (200, not a redirect) because an earlier `actingAs` in the same test persists for the rest of it — split into its own test rather than papering over it. **BROWSER-VERIFIED end to end** (org_admin `andrea.lindenhof`, deliberately un-enrolled to reach the screen): password alone still landed on `/two-factor/enrollment` (gate intact); the reveal printed `6NB2 · 4F23 · 6MSX · GH2C`, **character-for-character the decrypted DB secret AND the value the QR provisioning URI encodes**; **enrolment was then COMPLETED using a TOTP derived from the DISPLAYED secret** — the strongest available proof that the fallback is the real provisioning key and not a decorative string (a wrong secret cannot produce an accepted code) — landing on `/app`; a second un-enrolment reproduced it with a different real secret (`BQL6 · TY7Z · K534 · 4G6V`); `/login`, `/forgot-password`, `/reset-password/{token}` all still **200**. **THE NINE-PAGE WIREFRAME-PARITY PASS IS COMPLETE** — Admin Settings · Approval Queue · Branches · Agent&Tool Config · Billing&AR · Allergy Alert (safe part) · AR Account Detail · Appointment Detail · Auth Screens; two High **live security defects** were found and fixed by the pass along the way (AUTH-SEC.1, AUTH-SEC.2), and the one item deliberately left open is the password-policy **product** decision. See [[Platform]] / [[D-160]].

- `(pending)` OPMODE.G1 (FIRST of the OPERATOR-MODE chain — the SECURITY CORE): Platform/app-layer — the **OperatorGrant model + the fail-closed grant-gated super-admin access invariant, REPLACING the blanket `Gate::before` bypass (regression-guarded)**. Closes the LIVE containment gap found by `docs/features/OPERATOR-MODE-MAP.md` §3.1. **Opening:** top commit `ecacce2` (the MAP), tree clean, CI green (`check` → success), Redis up. **THE GAP (live, and independent of the feature):** `Gate::before` returned `true` **UNCONDITIONALLY** for any super-admin (`tenant_id === null`), and `PermissionService::has()` did the same for `hasPermission()` — TWO bypass points, not one. The only thing containing a super-admin was that `IdentifyTenantFromUser` never sets a tenant context for them, so `TenantScope` throws — **containment by accident, not by decision.** Operator Mode's core action is precisely to give an operator a tenant context, so on the old code the first line of G2 would have converted the accident into unlimited, unscoped, untimed, unaudited access to every record in that clinic. Hence the security core lands FIRST, before any request flow, approval or screen. **BUILT (additive):** migration `operator_access_grants` — deliberately **NOT `BelongsToTenant`** (a platform row REFERENCING a tenant, because the grant is what PRODUCES the context; scoping it would be circular), `dateTime()` moments (P0P.G15), operator/tenant/tier/scope-json/reason/status/granted_at/expires_at/revoked_at/revoked_by; model `OperatorGrant` (three tiers `read_only|configuration|full_support`, `isActiveAt()` re-checking status+revocation+expiry, `coversResource()`, **immutable grant FACTS** + never-deleted, enforced in `booted()`); `OperatorAccessService` (Platform — the single decision point); `OperatorGrantService` (`app/` — the BreakGlass layering, since Platform must not depend on Audit, D-017). **THE INVARIANT:** a platform operator reaches a tenant's data ONLY through a grant for THAT tenant that is **ACTIVE, UNEXPIRED, IN-TIER and IN-SCOPE** — enforced server-side at **BOTH** bypass points (fixing only the Gate would have left `hasPermission()` as a trivial sidestep). **CONTEXT-SENSITIVE, NOT A REMOVAL — this is what makes it surgical:** with NO tenant context the bypass stands (genuine platform work — the `/admin` console, the tenant list, cron, system jobs; no tenant row is reachable there anyway), and with a tenant context SET the blanket bypass is gone. Every legitimate super-admin path keeps working and the gap closes exactly where it was. **FAIL-CLOSED BY CONSTRUCTION:** tiers are an **ALLOW-LIST** (an ability no tier names is DENIED, so a permission added tomorrow is outside every operator tier until deliberately placed); `read_only` (billing/reporting/audit **view**) and `configuration` (+ admin/ai/comms **manage**) can NEVER reach PHI; the six PHI abilities need `full_support` **AND** the specific record id in the grant's scope, re-checked **AT ACCESS TIME** rather than once at session start; a grant with no expiry is invalid, not eternal; status/revocation/expiry are re-read on EVERY check so expiry and revoke bite on the very NEXT call (no cache to bust, no session to invalidate). **NOT THE BREAKGLASS MODEL:** `BreakGlassService::request()` sets `activated => true` — requesting IS granting, the one property Operator Mode must not have. Reused instead: its AUDIT discipline (required reason + append-only hash-chained row per transition) and its app-layer placement. `full_support` cannot be issued without an approver who is a user of the TARGET tenant and is NOT the operator (T6, enforced in the service, not a UI rule). **AUDIT:** every transition + `recordAccess()` writes to the **TARGET TENANT's** append-only hash-chained ledger under a NEW `actor_type = 'operator'`, so the clinic can single out platform activity in its own audit view; the chain still verifies. **TESTS (14 ADDED):** `tests/Feature/Platform/OperatorGrantAccessTest.php` — **THE REGRESSION GUARD** (a super-admin with NO grant in a tenant context is DENIED at both bypass points; and the bypass is proven context-sensitive rather than removed, so platform level still works); tier proofs (read_only refuses writes AND PHI; configuration adds config writes but still refuses PHI — T5); **scope at access time** (the 3 enumerated records open, everything else refused, a PHI ability with no resource id reaches nothing — T4); the allow-list property (an unplaced permission is denied); **EXPIRED grants nothing** (clock walked past `expires_at`, no job run — T2); **REVOKED grants nothing on the next check** (T3); a grant is usable only in the tenant and by the operator it names; **self-approval impossible** (no approver / self / another operator / a different tenant's user all refused, nothing created — T6); reason+time-box+operator-only required; **grant FACTS immutable** (tier/scope/expiry/tenant/operator all refused, delete refused); **THE AGENT EXCLUSION** (exact-file-list assertion + no AiCore/AiTool reference + nothing scheduled — T9); and the two-sided audit with the chain intact. **BUG FOUND + FIXED IN-GATE (a false green):** the agent-exclusion test first used `shell_exec('grep …')`, which FAILED on Windows and so passed for the wrong reason — replaced with a PHP-native recursive scan that behaves identically on CI Linux, plus an explicit `is_dir()` assertion so an empty result can never mean "the scan did not run". **ONE FLAGGED TEST CORRECTION (a security correction, NOT a weakening):** `RbacTest`'s *"a super-admin bypasses all checks via `Gate::before`"* documented the OLD blanket behaviour; its assertions are **unchanged** (they always ran at PLATFORM level, which is still a bypass), it was RENAMED to say so, and a second test pins the in-tenant denial. No other existing test was modified. **NO request flow, NO owner approval, NO route, NO UI** (G2/G3+) — there is no HTTP path to any of this yet, and the MAP's open product decisions (does Operator Mode ship at all; does the self-granted `configuration` WRITE tier stand) are untouched. Module boundaries intact (Platform still does not import Audit; 20 arch tests green). **A REGRESSION I INTRODUCED, CAUGHT BY `composer check` AND FIXED IN-GATE (15th test added):** the Horizon-dashboard guard (`viewHorizon`, short-circuited by `Gate::before`) went RED — a super-admin got 403 instead of 200. Cause: `TenantContext` is a request/job SINGLETON, so in a test that drives a tenant-staff request and then a super-admin request in the same container, the staff request LEFT a tenant context behind and my new rule then demanded a grant. Production could not hit it (fresh container per request), but the middleware only ASSUMED a super-admin has no context rather than enforcing it. Fixed properly at the source rather than in the test: `IdentifyTenantFromUser` now explicitly `forget()`s the context for a non-tenant-staff user, so its own documented contract ("super-admin -> leave TenantContext empty") is enforced, and an inherited context can now only ever DENY, never widen. **No existing test was touched to make this pass.** Also caught in-gate: the agent-exclusion exact-file-list assertion correctly FAILED when the middleware gained a comment naming the invariant — the list was widened deliberately, which is the assertion doing its job. See [[Platform]] / [[D-161]].
- `(pending)` OPMODE.G2 (SECOND of the OPERATOR-MODE chain): Platform/app-layer — **the operator REQUEST flow, whose defining property is that REQUESTING IS NOT GRANTING**. Per `docs/features/OPERATOR-MODE-MAP.md` §2 (the REQUESTED/PENDING states + the two TTL clocks). **Opening:** top commit `41a8dea` (OPMODE.G1), tree clean, CI green (`check` → success), Redis up. **STUDY:** G1's `issue()` creates an ACTIVE row directly (the guarded post-decision path); `status` already defaulted to `pending` but nothing produced one; `expires_at` was the SESSION TTL and there was no request clock, no `justification` column (G1's `reason` serves as it), and `granted_at`/`expires_at` were STRICTLY immutable. **TWO PRODUCT DECISIONS SETTLED (the map's §7 blockers, now built to):** (1) **`configuration` REQUIRES OWNER APPROVAL** — the wireframes drew it self-granted and the map flagged that as the design's weakest point, since it is a WRITE tier changing a live clinic's settings + agent config; `TIERS_REQUIRING_APPROVAL` is now `[configuration, full_support]`. (2) **`read_only` SELF-GRANTS**, defensible only because it is non-PHI: the G1 tier allow-list gives it exactly billing/reporting/audit **view** and refuses every PHI ability, so self-granting it cannot expose a record. **BUILT (additive):** migration `add_request_columns_to_operator_access_grants` — `requested_at`, **`request_expires_at`** (THE REQUEST CLOCK, kept as a SEPARATE COLUMN from the session `expires_at` on purpose — conflating the two is the classic bug in this shape of flow), `requested_ttl_minutes` (the session length ASKED FOR, applied only at approval), all `dateTime()` (P0P.G15) + a `[status, request_expires_at]` index for the sweeper; `OperatorGrantService::request()` (the entry point), `expireDueRequests()` (the request-TTL sweeper), `assertActivatable()` (**the guard G3's approve() MUST call**, written here so the rule cannot be re-invented alongside the endpoint later); model helpers `requiresApproval()`, `isRequestExpiredAt()`, `isAwaitingDecisionAt()`, and `TIERS_SELF_GRANTED`. **THE CORE PROPERTY — this is why BreakGlass was the wrong model to extend** (there, `request()` sets `activated => true`, so asking IS receiving): an approval-tier request creates a **PENDING** row with **NO `granted_at` and NO `expires_at`** — there is no session to be active with — so G1's invariant (which requires `status === active`) denies **EVERY** ability, including the very records the request names. Proven by driving all twelve tier abilities against a pending full_support request and getting `false` from each, plus `hasPermission()`. **NO SELF-APPROVAL PATH EXISTS:** nothing an operator can call moves their own pending request to active; the only method producing an active approval-tier grant is `issue()`, which demands an approver belonging to the target tenant who is not the operator (T6). A test also pins the **ABSENCE** of any `approve`/`selfApprove`/`activate`/`grant` verb on the service, so the gap is asserted rather than merely currently true. **THE REQUEST CLOCK GRANTS NOTHING:** an expired request is refused by `isAwaitingDecisionAt()` and by `assertActivatable()`, opens nothing before OR after the sweeper runs, and still has null `granted_at`/`expires_at` afterwards; the sweeper is housekeeping that makes the lapse visible and auditable, never the thing keeping access closed. **SCOPE MINIMISATION:** a `full_support` request must NAME its records (the map's "3 records tied to a ticket, not your whole database") — `*`, `all`, `ALL`, `any`, `%`, empty lists and blank ids are ALL refused, and **no wildcard exists at all**; whether an "all patient records" scope should exist is still an open product decision, so until it is answered the only way to reach a record is to have named it (fail-closed by omission, not by a flag someone could flip). **AUDIT:** `operator.access_requested` / `operator.self_granted` / `operator.request_expired` into the TARGET TENANT's append-only hash-chained ledger as `actor_type='operator'`, each carrying `grants_access_now` + `awaiting_owner_decision` so a row states plainly whether it granted anything; the chain still verifies. **ONE NARROWING OF A G1 RULE, recorded honestly:** G1 made `granted_at`/`expires_at` ABSOLUTELY immutable, which the pending→active transition cannot satisfy (a pending request has no clock and approval must start one) — they are now **SET-ONCE**, fillable from null exactly once and never rewritten, which is STRICTER where it matters (an existing session can never be silently re-clocked or extended by re-pointing the column) and is the minimum G3 needs; the request facts join the strictly-immutable set. **ONE FLAGGED CONTRACT CHANGE (not a weakening):** G1's *"a configuration grant adds config writes but still refuses PHI"* now passes an approver, because decision (1) made `configuration` owner-gated — its **ASSERTIONS are unchanged** (what the tier permits and refuses is identical; only how it comes into existence got stricter). The agent-exclusion exact-file-list also gained the new migration. No other existing test was modified. **TESTS (12 ADDED):** `tests/Feature/Platform/OperatorRequestFlowTest.php` — a full_support request is PENDING and opens NO access (all 12 abilities denied); a configuration request is PENDING too (D-162); a read_only request self-grants but permits ONLY non-PHI reads (writes AND PHI both denied); **NO self-approval** (self, and another platform operator, both refused; the request stays pending; the verb absent); an **EXPIRED request can never be approved or activated**; the sweeper leaves in-time requests and self-granted sessions alone; scope minimisation (no wildcard, no empty, no blank; named ids recorded verbatim); the request-input guards; the request audited as a REQUEST not an access; the self-grant + lapse audited; the session clock set ONCE; and the agent exclusion. **NOT IN THIS GATE:** no owner approval, no notification, no session mechanics beyond G1, **no route and no UI** — the flow is service-level only, so there is still no HTTP path to Operator Mode. **Still open:** whether Operator Mode ships in the first deployment at all, who counts as an "owner", and whether an "all patient records" scope is permitted. `composer check` FULLY green from the LOG TEXT (Pint `passed`, PHPStan L5 `[OK] No errors`, **Pest 1237 passed / 16257 assertions**); `composer test:smoke` green (4/4). See [[Platform]] / [[D-162]].
- `(pending)` OPMODE.G3 (THIRD of the OPERATOR-MODE chain): Platform/app-layer/Comms — **owner notification + the APPROVE / DOWNGRADE / DECLINE decision: the owner is the gate**. Per `docs/features/OPERATOR-MODE-MAP.md` (the owner-decision transitions). **Opening:** top commit `0afaa67` (OPMODE.G2), tree clean, CI green (`check` → success), Redis up. **STUDY:** G2's `request()` leaves an approval-tier row PENDING with no session clock; `assertActivatable()` was written in G2 precisely for this moment; `granted_at`/`expires_at` are SET-ONCE from null; `NotificationService::send(templateKey, Patient|User, …)` accepts a staff `User` and is never consent-gated for staff; a tenant's admins are `role_user` rows (NOT `role_assignments` — the table is named `role_user`) joined to a tenant-scoped `Role` with `key = org_admin`. **SETTLED PRODUCT DECISION (D-163): the OWNER *is* the tenant's `org_admin`.** No new role — `org_admin` already means "runs this clinic", and a parallel owner concept would have been a second, weaker path to the same authority. A tenant may hold several, so the wireframes' "+1 other owner can approve" falls out for free. **BUILT (additive):** migration `add_decision_columns_to_operator_access_grants` — `decided_at`/`decided_by`/`decision_note`/`supersedes_id` (all `dateTime()` where temporal, P0P.G15); model `OperatorGrant::TIER_RANK` + `OWNER_ROLE_KEY` + `supersedes()` + **`isNarrowerOrEqual()`**; service `ownersFor()`, `isOwnerOf()`, **`approve()`**, **`decline()`**, `notifyOwners()`, `recordDecision()`; a new `operator.access_requested` BUILT_IN notification template. **ONLY A TARGET-TENANT org_admin MAY DECIDE — fail-closed, server-side:** `isOwnerOf()` refuses a super-admin OUTRIGHT (so the operator can never decide their own request — the G2 rule now enforced from the other side too), refuses an org_admin of a DIFFERENT tenant, and refuses a tenant user without the role; every refusal leaves the request pending and opens nothing. **APPROVAL IS THE ONLY pending→active PATH, asserted STRUCTURALLY:** exactly TWO files in `Modules/` + `app/` may even name `OperatorGrant::STATUS_ACTIVE` (the access service reads it; the grant service writes it), and within the grant service there are exactly FOUR writes, each accounted for (self-granted `read_only`, `approve()` in place, `approve()`'s downgraded grant, `issue()`). No controller, job, command, agent or model callback can activate a grant. **A DOWNGRADE SUPERSEDES, IT NEVER MUTATES — the design decision of the gate:** G1/G2 make a grant's facts permanently immutable, and that rule is NOT bent here. Granting less closes the request as `declined` and creates a NEW active grant at the narrower tier/scope with `supersedes_id` pointing back at it (the ARDETAIL.P6 withdrawal recipe), so BOTH facts survive permanently — which is exactly what the wireframe shows the operator: *"YOU REQUESTED Full support / INSTEAD OWNER GRANTED Read-only"*. **AN OWNER MAY ONLY EVER NARROW:** `isNarrowerOrEqual()` enforces both axes — the granted tier's rank must be ≤ the requested tier's (`read_only` 0 < `configuration` 1 < `full_support` 2), and every granted id must ALREADY appear in the request, per kind, so a kind the request never mentioned cannot be introduced by the decision. A widening "downgrade" is refused and the request stays pending. **DECLINE + EXPIRY ACTIVATE NOTHING:** a decline is terminal, so `assertActivatable()` refuses it thereafter — no second bite in either direction; an already-approved request cannot be re-decided or silently re-clocked; an expired request cannot be approved at all. **THE OWNER IS NOTIFIED AND CANNOT BE MUTED:** every org_admin of the target tenant receives the operator, tier, the NAMED records, the justification and the request expiry (the two-party transparency the MAP describes); the template is deliberately ABSENT from `NotificationPreferenceService::MANAGEABLE`, and since only MANAGEABLE keys are ever written as preferences, a governance request is always ON and no admin screen can switch it off. **HONEST ABOUT THE CHANNEL:** the MAP draws in-app + email + push — **only EMAIL exists** (the standing SETTINGS.P5 seam), so only email is sent and only email is claimed. **NO OWNER ⇒ FAIL-CLOSED:** a tenant with no org_admin gets `operator.owner_unreachable` in its ledger and the request simply waits and lapses; it never self-approves for want of someone to ask. **TWO-SIDED AUDIT:** `operator.request_approved` / `_downgraded` / `_declined` are recorded with the **OWNER as actor** (`actor_type='user'`), not the operator — the clinic's own ledger showing its own admin deciding, beside the operator's request; the chain still verifies. **TESTS (13 ADDED):** `tests/Feature/Platform/OperatorOwnerDecisionTest.php` — an owner approval activates and then permits exactly its tier+scope (G1 enforcing); **approval is the ONLY pending→active path (structural)**; **ONLY the target-tenant org_admin can decide** (operator, a second operator, a foreign org_admin and plain staff ALL refused, request untouched, nothing opened); a downgrade grants less and SUPERSEDES rather than mutating; a downgrade may narrow the scope to a subset; a WIDENING downgrade is refused on both axes; decline activates nothing and is terminal; an already-approved request cannot be re-decided; an expired request cannot be approved; owners are notified with the full detail; a self-granted `read_only` request notifies nobody; the notification cannot be switched off and an ownerless tenant fails closed; the two-sided audit with the chain intact. **THREE TEST-SIDE BUGS FOUND + FIXED IN-GATE:** the assignment table is `role_user` (not `role_assignments`) so the qualified `where` was wrong; `NotificationDelivery`'s column is `rendered_body` (not `body`); and the structural assertion first searched for the bare `STATUS_ACTIVE`, which matched ~30 unrelated models (PaymentPlan, Allergy, Agent…) — narrowed to the QUALIFIED `OperatorGrant::STATUS_ACTIVE`. **ONE FLAGGED CONTRACT CHANGE (a strengthening, not a weakening):** G2's no-self-approval test asserted that NO `approve` verb existed, which was true while nothing could decide; G3 adds `approve()`, so the assertion became the stronger one it was always reaching for — the operator still cannot activate their own request, now because the decision demands a target-tenant org_admin (`selfApprove`/`activate`/`grant` still do not exist). The agent-exclusion exact-file-list also gained the new migration. No other existing test was modified. **NOT IN THIS GATE:** no elevated-session mechanics beyond G1's invariant (G4), **no route and no UI** (G6+) — there is still no HTTP path to Operator Mode. **Still open:** whether Operator Mode ships in the first deployment at all, and whether an "all patient records" scope is ever permitted. `composer check` FULLY green from the LOG TEXT (Pint `passed`, PHPStan L5 `[OK] No errors`, **Pest 1250 passed / 16351 assertions**); `composer test:smoke` green (4/4). See [[Platform]] / [[D-163]].
- `(pending)` **MEMORY RECONCILIATION after PAUSING Operator Mode mid-feature (as of OPMODE.G3, HEAD `c086de5`).** Docs/memory + a migration run only — NO feature work, no gate. **Opening verified:** HEAD `c086de5` (OPMODE.G3), tree clean, `main...origin/main` up to date, CI `check -> completed / success` for that SHA. **VERIFIED AGAINST REPO GROUND TRUTH:** all three Operator Mode commits exist and match their descriptions (`41a8dea` G1, `0afaa67` G2, `c086de5` G3); the feature is **10 tracked files** (3 migrations + model + access service + grant service + the MAP + 3 test files); the three suites hold **15 + 12 + 13 = 40 tests** (counted, not assumed); and **`grep` over `routes/*.php` finds ZERO Operator Mode routes** — the only "operator" hit is an unrelated Betreibung comment, confirming the feature is **backend-only and inert with no HTTP surface**. **THE POINT OF THIS RECONCILIATION — the state is DELIBERATE, not half-built:** G1–G3 are DONE and LIVE-SAFE and shipped a **real security fix** (the live super-admin containment gap is CLOSED — `Gate::before` AND `PermissionService::has()` both returned an unconditional `true`, and the only thing containing a super-admin was never being handed a tenant context); **G4–G11 are DELIBERATELY DEFERRED to post-first-customer** as operator-facing convenience UI that adds no safety property G1–G3 do not already enforce. Recorded so no future session mistakes the pause for unfinished work or "finishes" it unprompted. **DISCREPANCY FOUND AND FIXED:** the task referred to `docs/wireframe-parity/WAITLIST-MANAGEMENT-DIFF.md` as existing — **it did NOT.** The Waitlist audit had been delivered as a conversation report and never written to a file, so the reference pointed at a phantom document. Written now, with every finding **re-verified against the code at `c086de5`** rather than from memory: `WaitlistService::create()` still has exactly ONE caller in the repo (`DemoClinicSeeder` — the blocker), the four waitlist routes are all POST with no create/index, `FillFromWaitlistTool` still carries `autonomyCeiling: APPROVE` (so the wireframe's auto-send tier must be OMITTED), and `waitlist.offer` is still `CHANNEL_EMAIL` only (SMS/phone are the SETTINGS.P5 seam). **RECONCILED:** **NEW `memory/modules/OperatorMode.md`** (the 21st module file — a cross-module feature note leading with an unmissable "the state is deliberate" banner: what is done and why it was worth doing ahead of deploy, exactly what G4–G11 would add, the consequences of the pause, the settled decisions, the one open scope question, key classes, and the four gotchas that each cost time once — `role_user` not `role_assignments`, `rendered_body` not `body`, the qualified `OperatorGrant::STATUS_ACTIVE`, and the T9 agent exclusion); **NEW `docs/wireframe-parity/WAITLIST-MANAGEMENT-DIFF.md`** (the missing audit, incl. the DECIDED SCOPE for a future chain and the one fence conflict); **`DECISIONS.md`** — appended **D-164** recording the deferral itself (D-161/162/163 already covered the built decisions; the *pause* was the one thing undocumented), audited clean at **D-001 → D-164, no gaps, no duplicates**; **`PROJECT-STATE.md`** — the Operator Mode section retitled from "IN PROGRESS" to **"⏸️ PAUSED AFTER ITS SECURITY CORE"** with a leading do-not-misread banner, plus a new section recording the two-wireframe triage (Waiting On Approval = NOT a parity page, folded into the Operator Mode MAP; Waitlist = a real page, audited, not started) and the DEPLOYMENT section reframed as the (a)–(g) priority list; **`DEFERRED.md`** — the header replaced with a **prioritised table (DEPLOY first)**, and the stale "blocked on a decision" Operator Mode section rewritten as "G4–G11 DELIBERATELY DEFERRED" alongside a new Waitlist section and a Waiting-On-Approval triage note; **`docs/ONBOARDING.md`** — D-001→D-164, the module count 20→21 with a pointer to `OperatorMode.md`, two new banners (Operator Mode paused / Waitlist audited-not-built) and the next-track section reframed as the priority list. **THE SINGLE HIGHEST-VALUE TRACK REMAINS DEPLOYMENT** — the runbook, `.env` template and rehearsed onboarding are ready, and the **undiagnosed staging error is still parked** and still expected as the first real step. **THE FIRST `composer check` RUN WAS RED, AND THE WRAPPER EXIT CODE LIED ABOUT IT AGAIN (exit 0 while the log said `error code 2`) — caught only by reading the log text.** The failure was `SurgicalItemUsageParallelHammerTest` throwing **`ProcessTimedOutException`** after 145s. Diagnosed rather than dismissed: the change surface is **7 files, ALL `.md`, zero code**; the failure is a timeout, not an assertion; and the test **passes in isolation at 137.8s**. Root cause is structural — the test spawns **8 concurrent full-app `artisan` boots with a 30-second PER-PROCESS ceiling** (`new Process(..., 30)`), which is marginal on a loaded Windows dev box; **there are 7 such parallel-hammer tests**, so this is a class-wide sensitivity, not one bad test. See [[OperatorMode]] / [[D-164]].
- `(pending)` DEPLOY.PROV (first of the DEPLOY track): Platform/app-layer/docs — **first-customer provisioning made REAL: `tenant:create` + `tenant:add-admin` + `plans:seed` + the `.env` MUST-FILL runbook section** (closes pre-deploy blockers M1–M4 from `docs/DEPLOY-READINESS-CHECK.md`). **Opening:** top commit `eebbe91` (the readiness check), tree clean, CI green, Redis up. **THE PROBLEM:** the readiness check found that what blocked the first paying customer was NOT the application but the **absence of any way to create one** — `Tenant::create` existed ONLY inside the three demo seeders (no route, no controller, no command), and there was **no `User::create` in production code at all** outside `StaffInviteService`, which needs an already-authenticated admin to send an invite, so the FIRST org_admin of a new tenant could not be invited by anyone. Both were closable only by undocumented Tinker. **BUILT (3 commands, NO HTTP surface — provisioning is an operator action on the box, not an endpoint):** `plans:seed` (the real subscription plans; idempotent via the existing `updateOrCreate`-on-`key` catalog; demo-free by construction); `tenant:create` (a REAL, MINIMAL tenant — name/slug/region/plan + locale/currency/timezone settings, seeding NO patients/staff/money; relies on the existing `Tenant::created` → `provisionTenant()` hook for the starter roles and then **VERIFIES the hook fired and reports the count** rather than assuming it; **REFUSES a duplicate slug and an unknown plan BEFORE any write**, so a re-run can never half-create or silently reattach); `tenant:add-admin` (**the FIRST org_admin — the one bootstrap that needs no existing user**; uses the REAL paths, `User::create` with the model's password hash-cast + `RoleAssignment::create` which fires the audited `role.assigned`, with `branch_id = null` because a branch-scoped assignment does not answer gate checks that pass no branch; **REFUSES a second admin** — once one exists there is somebody who can invite the next, so the bootstrap steps aside rather than becoming a permanent second user-creation path — and refuses a duplicate email; generates and prints a one-time temporary password when none is given). **NOTHING WEAKENED:** mandatory 2FA still applies to the bootstrapped admin (the command creates NO `two_factor_secret`, so `EnsureTwoFactorEnabled` forces enrolment on first login and they cannot reach the app until they enrol — asserted by test); tenancy stays fail-closed (tenant-scoped writes run inside an explicitly set context that is **restored afterwards**, so a caller's context is never clobbered); console writes audit as `actor_type = service`, which is honest for an operator-run command; and the demo-seeder safety is untouched. **M3 — THE SILENT FAILURE, FIXED AT THE ROOT:** `tenants.plan_id` is nullable and `FeatureService` falls through to `false` for every feature when a tenant has no plan, so on a fresh production DB (where the runbook never ran `db:seed`) telehealth/EVV/ai_drafting were all quietly OFF with nothing to indicate why. The runbook's release sequence now seeds the catalogs, `plans:seed` makes it explicit and idempotent, and `tenant:create` takes a plan and warns loudly when asked for none. **M4 — the runbook now carries a MUST-FILL `.env` section**: the 13 REQUIRED keys each with its REAL code default and actual failure mode (`DB_CONNECTION` defaults to **sqlite**; `QUEUE_CONNECTION` defaults to `database` so **Horizon reports healthy and processes nothing**; `MAIL_MAILER` defaults to **log**, silently dropping password resets, invites, reminders, dunning and the email-only operator owner-approval notice; `SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE` has **no default at all** so the cookie is not marked Secure), plus REQUIRED-IF-USED (`LIVEKIT_HOST` — not `LIVEKIT_URL`; `MAIL_SCHEME` not `MAIL_ENCRYPTION`) and OPTIONAL-degrade-gracefully (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` — the app boots fine without it). The runbook also replaces the old "Create their tenant" hand-wave with the two real commands, and gains a "three things that are easy to skip" note (build BOTH bundles ON THE SERVER since they are gitignored; Horizon AND the scheduler are both required daemons; seed the catalogs). **A DOCUMENTATION DRIFT CAUGHT BY ACTUALLY RUNNING IT:** the live `tenant:create` reported **26** starter role templates, not the **17** claimed by the runbook — and by my own readiness check, which had inherited the number from the runbook instead of counting. Nine hospital roles had landed since that line was written. Both documents corrected, and the test asserts `count(RbacProvisioner::ROLE_TEMPLATES)` so it cannot drift again. **TESTS (9 ADDED):** `tests/Feature/Platform/ProvisioningCommandsTest.php` — a real tenant with every starter role template seeded (the hook fired) + settings written + no demo data; duplicate slug REFUSED without half-creating; input validation and an unknown plan refused with nothing created; **the FIRST org_admin created with ZERO pre-existing users** (precisely what StaffInviteService cannot do) with the org_admin role across all branches and `admin.manage` resolving through the real Gate; a second admin REFUSED; unknown tenant / bad email / duplicate email all refused; **the bootstrapped admin is still forced to 2FA enrolment** (no secret, redirected to enrolment, enrolment reachable); `plans:seed` idempotent and **FeatureService then resolves REAL features** (eu_pro → telehealth+ai_drafting on, evv honestly off; eu_starter → telehealth off) instead of a blanket false; and **the production seed path cannot reach a demo seeder** (`DatabaseSeeder` contains no `Demo`, `db:seed --force` creates zero tenants but does seed the catalogs). **VERIFIED END-TO-END ON THE DEV DB**, not just in tests: `plans:seed` → 2 plans; `tenant:create "Readiness Probe Clinic"` → 26 roles seeded, plan eu_pro; `tenant:add-admin` → org_admin created with a one-time password and the 2FA notice. **THE READINESS VERDICT MOVES FROM 🟡 CONDITIONAL GO TO 🟢 GO** — M1–M4 resolved; S1 (unscheduled audit partitions, which degrade rather than fail thanks to the `p_max` catch-all) and S2 (unguarded demo seeders, mitigated by their absence from `DatabaseSeeder`) remain open, non-blocking, and are now explicit DEFERRED items. `composer check` FULLY green from the LOG TEXT (Pint `passed`, PHPStan L5 `[OK] No errors`, **Pest 1259 passed / 16425 assertions**); `composer test:smoke` green (4/4). See [[Platform]] / [[D-165]].
- `506762a` DENTAL-B.P1 (first of the DENTAL-B chain): Dental/frontend — the **shared dental components**, purely presentational (P0D.GU), so P2–P6 reuse instead of re-solving each screen. **Opening:** top commit `ef83b4e` (the dental batch diff), CI green, Redis up, DemoClinicSeeder + DemoDentalSeeder (Zahnarztpraxis Morgenstern). **BUILT** under a new `resources/js/Components/Dental/` namespace: **S1 `PatientClinicalHeader.vue`** (identity + MRN + DOB/age/sex + the RECORDED allergy chip + patient-360 link; `age`/`dateOfBirth` arrive ALREADY FORMATTED so the component never constructs a `Date` — D-091 — and never subtracts dates; the chip's styling is CONSTANT, recorded severity is TEXT and drives no class/colour/ordering); **S2 `ToothArch.vue` + `toothConditionColour.ts`** (the FDI arch EXTRACTED from `Odontogram.vue`, not rewritten — same ordering, mini-diagram, selection ring, missing-tooth strike-through, chart key and scoped styles, moved wholesale; the *"FACTUAL charted-condition legend (categorical, NOT a severity ramp) … No score/grade/gradient anywhere"* contract moved VERBATIM so all six arch-drawing screens inherit ONE categorical vocabulary; a multi-root fragment keeps the DOM identical under the parent's `space-y-6`); **S4 `ClinicalStatTile.vue`** (the tile SHELL only, deliberately CLOSED — label + caller-supplied value STRING + optional unit/caption, **no slot, no tone/colour/status/trend/direction prop, no arithmetic, no threshold comparison**; the audit flagged the tiles as where the fence gets breached, since the mock fills this exact tile with BOP %, DMFT, mean pocket depth, "1 finding", "one site to watch", "▼ from 3.1"; it intentionally does NOT reuse the generic `StatCard.vue`, whose icon slot would reopen the hole); **S5 `ProcedureCard.vue`** (code/name/tooth/surface/status chrome; `amount` is an already-formatted ENGINE-SUPPLIED string — no minor-unit division, no summing, no totalling); plus a unique `dentalShared` i18n block (no dotted keys, no `@`). **Only Odontogram was rewired** (to S2), proving the extraction; S1/S4/S5 ship registered but unconsumed for P2–P6, exactly as scoped. **S3 WAS ALREADY BUILT — corrected the audit rather than duplicating it:** the tab strip is `Components/DentalSectionNav.vue` from DENTAL.G9 (five tabs, real routes, i18n'd); its per-tab role-gating was examined and DELIBERATELY left alone because all five targets are gated identically at `patient.view`, so per-tab gating would invent a distinction the routes do not make (`/dental/fee-schedule` is `billing.manage` and correctly absent). §3's "six components" also overcounted — S6 (scan tile) was never in P1's scope; **four new components delivered (S1/S2/S4/S5), S3 pre-existing**. **THE EXTRACTION IS PROVEN BEHAVIOUR-IDENTICAL, NOT ASSUMED:** the Odontogram chart card was captured from a real browser before and after — **18109 normalised chars, byte-for-byte IDENTICAL**; all 32 teeth identical on number, classes, computed opacity, all five per-surface background colours and the whole-tooth mark; full-page text identical; click-to-select still yields exactly one `.tooth-selected` with the scoped outline intact. **NO existing test modified** — the 58 `tests/Feature/Dental` tests, the Evals and the Architecture suite stayed green untouched. **Tests added:** `tests/Feature/Dental/SharedComponentsTest.php` (8 tests, 291 assertions), STRUCTURAL by design because the property under test is an ABSENCE (a render test covers today's callers, not the affordance the next caller reaches for); it scans **comment-stripped** source because the components' comments deliberately NAME what they refuse to build ("DMFT", "BOP %", "trend arrows") and that documentation must not be what trips the scan; **the assertions were MUTATION-CHECKED** — a `trend` prop, a `Number(value) / 100`, and a `:class` keyed to `allergy.severity` each turn the suite red; `severity` is the ONE narrow documented allowance, only in `PatientClinicalHeader.vue`. **§5.1 stays out, §6 stays in:** no DMFT, finding count, site-to-watch, index, trend, colour band or AI finding reintroduced (asserted for both page and widget), and the on-screen chart-key note ("not its severity") is re-asserted. Two test-side bugs found and fixed before green: the map-entry count assumed quoted JS keys (they are unquoted), and `baseline` matched the Tailwind `items-baseline` class — dropped from the forbidden list with a comment, since listing a CSS utility teaches the next author to weaken the scan rather than respect it. **VERIFY:** `npm run build` green · Pint passed · PHPStan L5 "No errors" (848 files) · vitest 29 passed · `composer test:smoke` 4 passed · Dental 58 passed · Evals+Architecture 57 passed · full `composer check` green.
