- `22a2a3d` PT.P1 (first of the PT chain): Portal/app-layer — **portal read-audit rows on six surfaces + the `/portal/login` guest-smoke line**; additive audit only, no visual work. **Opening:** top commit `d2e4f7c` (the portal audit), tree clean, Redis PONG, CI `completed / success` confirmed BEFORE starting. Fixture verified BY QUERY: Erika Baumgartner (active portal account, `portal.access` + `comms.email` granted, 1 appointment · 1 shared document · 2 issued invoices · 2 threads · 2 consents) plus the CONTROL patient Viktor Odermatt (3 issued invoices, NO portal account) whose log must stay empty. **THE ESTABLISHED PATTERN, MATCHED VERBATIM:** `$model->auditRead(['surface' => '…'])` → `AuditService::recordRead()`, which stamps actor + actor_type from `PlatformAuditContext` and the patient id from `LogsReads::auditPatientId()`. The subject is the **Patient** (its `auditPatientId()` returns its own id), which is exactly what makes the row selectable by PC.P5's query (`action='read' AND patient_id = ?`) — the same shape `patient_360` (PC.P1), `referrals` (PC.P6) and `recall_worklist` (PC.P7) already use. **SIX SURFACES AUDITED:** `portal_home`, `portal_appointments`, `portal_documents`, `portal_invoices`, `portal_consents`, `portal_checkin`. **ALREADY AUDITED AND LEFT ALONE (no double-audit):** the per-document DOWNLOAD (`portal_document_download`), the per-invoice PDF DOWNLOAD (`portal_invoice_download`), the message-thread open (`comms_thread_portal`, in ThreadService) and the telehealth token issue (`telehealth_token`, in TelehealthService); `PortalTreatmentPlanController` already audits per plan and was untouched. `updateContact` is a pure write and is deliberately NOT read-audited; the existing `appointment.checked_in` ACTION row is a different action, not a second read path. **NOTHING ELSE CHANGED** — no query, no scoping, no disclosure, no behaviour. **PROVEN END TO END IN A BROWSER:** signed in as the real portal patient, visited all five page surfaces, then opened that patient's PC.P5 access log as staff — **all five appear on screen AND in the CSV export** (same query, so they cannot disagree), with the actor-type chip reading **"Patient (self) · 6"**. The `portal_home · patient` row the PC.P5 wireframe draws is now real. **A TEST ARTIFACT THAT NEARLY BECAME A WRONG ASSERTION:** the suite first reported `actor_type = user` for portal reads. `actingAs($account, 'patient')` also calls `shouldUse('patient')`, so `Auth::user()` resolves the PortalAccount — and `PlatformAuditContext::actor()` checks `Auth::user()` FIRST. In production the default guard stays `web`, `Auth::user()` is null, and it falls through to the patient branch. **I checked production in the browser BEFORE touching the assertion** (six rows, all `actor_type=patient`), then rewired the test to drive the guard the way a real request does via `Auth::guard('patient')->setUser()`. The assertion was right; the test's driving was wrong. **THE SMOKE LINE, PROVEN TO BITE:** `/portal/login` added to the AUTH-SEC.2 guest-route list — the patient's own entry point, which sat outside the very test written because `/forgot-password` shipped a 500 unnoticed. Breaking the route's resolution turned the guest smoke red with **`guest.portal.login [/portal/login] -> 500`**, then it was restored. **The guest list was EXTENDED, never relaxed** (the one existing behaviour test touched this gate — flagged deliberately: strictly more to satisfy, nothing weakened). **Tests added:** `tests/Feature/Patients/PortalReadAuditTest.php` (5 tests, 52 assertions) — one row per render per surface (asserted at 0 → 1 → 2 across two renders), check-in one row per request, the row's `actor_type`/`actor_id`/`resource_type`/`patient_id`, visibility in BOTH the PC.P5 report and its export, the CONTROL patient's log empty **per surface**, and the guest-smoke list containing `/portal/login`. **MUTATION-CHECKED FOUR WAYS — and one of my own mutations proved NOTHING until it was fixed.** A double audit on Home, the consents audit removed, and `portal.login` dropped from the smoke all turn the suite red. But "audit the invoices row against another patient" was written as `Patient::orderBy('id')->first()`, which with ULIDs resolves back to the VIEWER — a **no-op mutation whose passing meant nothing**. Rewritten as `where('id', '!=', $account->patient_id)` it turns the suite red; the guard was strengthened in the same pass so the control patient is asserted empty **per surface** rather than once at the end. **Own slips:** Pint flagged an import order and an unused import; a scratch regex broke on `\P` (unicode-property escape) and was replaced with plain string replacement. **VERIFY:** no frontend touched (no `npm run build` needed) · Pint passed · PHPStan L5 "No errors" · Patients+Smoke 58 passed · full `composer check` green · `composer test:smoke` green incl. the new guest line. Docs: `PORTAL-BATCH-DIFF.md` §4a outcome + PT.P1 marked DONE (B1 + B4 closed).
- `0ab2b63` PT.P1-FIX (follow-up to PT.P1 `22a2a3d`, which went **RED ON CI**): the six audit rows and the smoke line were correct — **my TEST matched the audit JSON as raw bytes**, and that is dialect-dependent. `ptaRows()` used `->where('context', 'like', '%"surface":"portal_home"%')`. **MySQL 8 re-serialises a JSON column on insert** (it stores `{"surface": "portal_home"}` — note the space after the colon — and may reorder keys), while local **MariaDB keeps the bytes it was handed**. So the pattern matched everything locally (1346 passed) and matched NOTHING on CI: `Failed asserting that 0 is identical to 1` on two tests. **This is the APPT.P2 lesson exactly — "assert audit JSON by DECODING it, never as text" — and I walked straight back into it.** The memory note existed; I did not apply it. **THE ROWS WERE ALWAYS BEING WRITTEN** — the browser proof (six rows, `actor_type=patient`, all five surfaces visible in the access log AND the CSV export, chip reading "Patient (self) · 6") was taken against a real server and stands unchanged. No app code was wrong; nothing about the gate's claims changes. **FIX:** all three raw-context matches in the test now DECODE the JSON and compare `$decoded['surface']`, which is engine-independent by construction. **AND THE FAILURE WAS TURNED INTO A PERMANENT GUARD:** a new test inserts the SAME fact under BOTH spellings (`{"surface":"portal_home"}` and `{"surface": "portal_home"}`) and requires the helper to count BOTH — so a future byte-matching helper fails on ANY engine, including MariaDB, instead of waiting for CI to catch it. Proven: reverting `ptaRows()` to the `LIKE` form turns that test red (`Failed asserting that 1 is identical to 2`). **VERIFY:** Pint passed · PHPStan L5 "No errors" · full `composer check` green · `composer test:smoke` green. **The standing rule, restated because I broke it: local green ≠ CI green, and audit JSON is the specific trap.**
- `59e025c` PT.P2 (second of the PT chain): Portal/Billing — **patient-facing chrome + the ONE-SOURCE open balance**. **Opening:** top commit `0ab2b63` (PT.P1-FIX), tree clean, Redis PONG, CI `completed / success` confirmed BEFORE starting. Fixture verified BY QUERY and made non-trivial on purpose (D-174): the portal patient with a FULLY OPEN invoice, a **PARTLY PAID** one (a real partial payment through `PaymentService::record()` + `allocate()`) and a SETTLED one — engine outstanding **21300 CHF** — so a wrong column, a dropped row or a filtered sum would each produce a visibly different number. **THE TWO SOURCES, STATED:** Portal Home summed `InvoiceBalance.open_balance_minor` over the patient's ISSUED invoices **server-side**; `Invoices.vue` ran `.reduce()` over the rows it had been sent **and excluded credit notes**. Not merely two derivations — two DIFFERENT RULES, so with a credit note on the account the two screens told the patient different things about what they owed. **THE AUTHORITY IS THE ENGINE:** `MetricsService::accountLedger()` defines `account_outstanding_minor` = Σ the projection's open balances and asserts a δ=0 tie against its own running balance; it does NOT exclude credit notes. Home already matched it; the Vue did not. **FIX:** new `PatientBalanceReader` (Modules/Billing) is the single place that answers "what does this patient owe" — `outstandingMinorFor()`, `currencyFor()`, `present()` and `format()`. Home and Invoices both read it; the `.reduce()` is deleted. **A test proves all THREE agree** (engine, Home, Invoices) and that the engine ledger itself ties. **THE SUBTLE PART, worth keeping:** the total must NOT be a sum of the rows because the page filters the list, and the wireframe promises "your open balance stays the full total" — a promise only keepable if the figure was never derived from the rows. **NO PAGE-SIDE MONEY ARITHMETIC ANYWHERE IN THE PORTAL:** money is formatted server-side (the DENTAL-B.P4 contract) — Home, Invoices and the portal Treatment Plan all lost their `/100`, and per-invoice `total`/`open_balance` strings now come from the reader. An adversarial test scans every portal page + portal component + the portal layout for `/100`, `minor /`, `reduce((sum` and `_minor * /`, with a positive control that the scan resolved the real files (D-173/D-174). **`Documents.vue` still divides for a FILE SIZE in MB — not money** — so the ban is written against the money idioms specifically rather than arithmetic in general, and that distinction is stated in the test. **THE PATIENT-FACING HEADER IS A NEW COMPONENT, NOT A STRETCHED S1 (D-181).** `PortalPageHeader` (+ `PortalEmptyState`) live in a new `Components/Portal/` namespace. It renders an eyebrow, a title and a lead line, and **deliberately does NOT render MRN, date of birth, sex, an allergy tile or a dark clinical surface** — that is S1's staff framing, and a patient does not need their own MRN quoted back at them. It carries no tone/severity/urgency prop, so nothing patient-facing can be tinted by a value. **S1 is untouched**; the test asserts both halves (the portal header renders no staff field; S1 never learns about the portal). **Note the contrast with PC.P3**, which EXTENDED S1 rather than forking it — right there because both callers were staff surfaces; wrong here because the caller is the patient. **ADOPTED ON DOCUMENTS AND CONSENTS, PROVEN BYTE-IDENTICAL** the PC.P1 way: the component's markup is the exact block those screens already rendered (a fragment root, so surrounding layout flow is unchanged too), and a browser capture before/after matched character for character — **326 → 326** and **352 → 352** chars, exact string equality. The other five auth screens were left alone this gate: their header blocks differ enough that adopting them is a visual decision rather than an extraction, and it belongs with PT.P3/PT.P4 where those screens are worked anyway. **Stated rather than quietly skipped.** **Tests added:** `tests/Feature/Billing/PortalBalanceOneSourceTest.php` (5 tests, 138 assertions) — engine/Home/Invoices agreement + the engine tie; the balance surviving a filtered list; the adversarial no-money-arithmetic scan; the patient header's presences AND absences with S1 untouched; and **PT.P1's audit rows still exactly one per render** (decoded, never byte-matched — the PT.P1-FIX lesson). **MUTATION-CHECKED SIX WAYS, all red:** Invoices diverging from the shared reader (2 tests), the client `.reduce()` reintroduced, a page-side divide reintroduced, the reader diverging from the engine (2 tests), the portal header growing an MRN, and Home's audit row removed. **Own slips, all caught before green:** the first fixture hand-built invoices and hit `payer_type doesn't have a default value` — rewritten to issue through the REAL charge → draft → issue path, which is also the more honest fixture; a payment currency hardcoded to CHF when the tenant setting decides it (now taken from the invoice); `accountLedger()`'s return keys are flat (`account_outstanding_minor`, `ties`) not nested under `summary`; Pint import order/unused imports; and PHPStan caught `$balances`/`$currency` not being passed into a closure. **BROWSER-VERIFIED:** all eight authenticated portal screens render 200 with a header; **Home and Invoices both show `CHF 213.00`**, tying to the engine's 21300 (δ=0); invoice rows render with **ONE distinct style** across open / partly-paid / settled — no overdue band, no severity tint (D-169). **VERIFY:** `npm run build` green · Pint passed · PHPStan L5 "No errors" · portal suites 11 passed · full `composer check` green · `composer test:smoke` green. Docs: `PORTAL-BATCH-DIFF.md` §4b outcome + PT.P2 marked DONE (B5 closed; B6 and the remaining chrome deferred to PT.P3/P4 and stated).
- `a144fcf` PT.P3 (third of the PT chain): Portal/Scheduling — **Portal Home + Appointments visual parity** over machinery the audit found contract-correct, P0D.GU. **Opening:** top commit `59e025c` (PT.P2), tree clean, Redis PONG, CI `completed / success` confirmed BEFORE starting. Fixture seeded and **verified BY QUERY**, representative (D-174): appointments spanning **completed · in_progress · confirmed (in 6h) · booked (in 9d) · cancelled**, 2 upcoming, 1 shared document (of 2 total — the unshared one is the control for the count), open balance 31300, one online-bookable branch, and a control patient with 0 appointments. **HOME:** counts are now **SERVER-computed row counts** (shared documents · upcoming appointments · unread messages) rather than a Vue length over a partial payload — the PC.P2 defect; the balance stays PT.P2's `PatientBalanceReader`, tying to the engine. The mock's 4-tile quick-actions row **already existed**; what was missing were the figures beside them. **APPOINTMENTS:** the card now carries the **RECORDED BRANCH** — a real column the payload had simply not included, and exactly what the wireframe shows beside the service. Groups, the server-enforced cancel window and the slot search were already contract-correct. `PortalPageHeader` adopted **byte-identically** (the header block was already that exact shape) and the three empty states now use `PortalEmptyState`. **A DELIBERATE NON-ADOPTION, STATED:** Home's greeting was NOT forced into `PortalPageHeader` — a greeting ("Good morning, Nora", large, no eyebrow) is a different treatment from a section header and the mock treats it as one. Shared chrome is worth having where the shape is genuinely shared, not applied everywhere for uniformity. **RESCHEDULE IS ABSENT AND STAYS ABSENT (D-170):** the portal has book / cancel / check-in and no reschedule route; none was invented, and a test asserts BOTH halves — the routes that exist (positive control) and the ones that must not. **THE BOOKING GUARD RE-ASSERTED — AND I NEARLY MIS-PROVED IT (D-182).** Verified in a browser AND in tests: the real `AvailableSlotFinder` returns 12 capped slots each carrying resource ids; a soft-suspended branch (BRANCH.P1 `accepts_online_bookings = false`) returns **an empty list, not an error**; a forged booking that skips the slot list is **refused**; and booking the same slot twice is refused by `lockResource` + `assertNoOverlap` — a patient cannot double-book. **BUT the soft-suspend assertion first PASSED WITH THE GUARD DELETED**: my suspended branch had no resources, so the finder returned `[]` either way and the test proved nothing. Once the suspended branch was given fully bookable practitioner + room resources with availability, the ONLY thing between the patient and a slot was the flag — and the mutation turned the suite red. **The rule: build a refusal fixture so that WITHOUT the guard it would SUCCEED** — D-174's positive control pointed at a refusal rather than a scan. **THE FENCE, SHARPENED RATHER THAN RELAXED:** the D-169 ban first fired on `selectedSlot?.starts_at === slot.starts_at`, which is **selection state** — which chip the patient clicked — not imminence. The rule now distinguishes IDENTITY from PROXIMITY: an equality between two slot ids is ordinary UI; what stays forbidden is chrome keyed to HOW SOON something is (a time compared relationally, against `now()`, or against a duration threshold) — the shape every "turn it amber as it approaches" implementation must take. Emphasising the FIRST upcoming row is positional, the same class as PC.P7's date sort, and stays. **Tests added:** `tests/Feature/Scheduling/PortalAppointmentsParityTest.php` (7 tests, 157 assertions) — Home's counts === real row counts (1 shared of 2 documents, so a wrong set reads 2) + the balance tying to the engine; the real spread in the right groups with the recorded branch and only online-bookable branches offered; the booking guard; the overlap guard; the D-170 reschedule absence; the fence across the spread; and **PT.P1's one row per render on both screens** (decoded, never byte-matched). **MUTATION-CHECKED FIVE WAYS — one of which exposed the fixture hole above:** an imminence tint (caught by the sharpened rule), a `noShowRisk` payload key, the BRANCH.P1 guard deleted (initially GREEN — fixed), a Home count over the wrong set, and Home's balance diverging from the reader. **Own slips:** the first fixture built Services and Resources without `code`/`category`/`requires_resource_types` and without `ResourceAvailability`, so the finder was empty; Pint flagged a phpdoc type. **BROWSER-VERIFIED:** Home renders **CHF 313.00** (= engine 31300) with the greeting and quick actions; Appointments shows all five appointments in the right groups with the branch and the "within 24 hours — please call the practice" line on the imminent one; the slot search returned **12 real finder slots**, the soft-suspended branch returned **0** and the forged booking was **refused**; no urgency/priority/no-show/"should rebook" language anywhere. **VERIFY:** `npm run build` green · Pint passed · PHPStan L5 "No errors" · Scheduling + portal suites 115 passed · full `composer check` green · `composer test:smoke` green. Docs: `PORTAL-BATCH-DIFF.md` §4c outcome + PT.P3 marked DONE (Appointments adopted PortalPageHeader + PortalEmptyState; Home adopted neither, and why).
- `ab72168` PT.P4 (fourth of the PT chain): Portal/Comms/Billing/Clinical — **Documents + Invoices + Messages visual parity**, and the three disclosure guards pinned properly. P0D.GU. **Opening:** top commit `a144fcf` (PT.P3), tree clean, Redis PONG, CI `completed / success` confirmed BEFORE starting. Fixture **verified BY QUERY** and built for D-182: an UNSHARED document **belonging to the portal patient** (so only `shared_with_patient` hides it), a SHARED document belonging to the CONTROL patient, a DRAFT invoice with a real total (so only `whereNotNull('number')` hides it) alongside an issued one, a thread the patient participates in and one they do not, an AI-ASSISTED staff reply, and a live session whose consent is then withdrawn. **MOST OF THESE SCREENS WERE ALREADY RIGHT:** documents are `shared_with_patient`-only and stream from the private disk with nosniff; invoices are issued-only with PT.P2's reader balance and no pay button; messages are scoped by `ThreadParticipant` membership. The visible change is small — **Messages adopted `PortalPageHeader` byte-identically**, and the recorded **`ai_assisted`** provenance now reaches the patient. **A DELIBERATE DISAGREEMENT WITH THE WIREFRAME, SURFACED NOT BURIED:** the mock says *"staff-side AI provenance (✦) never crosses to the portal"*; this gate said show provenance as recorded. **I followed the gate but phrased it to keep accountability exact.** `ai_assisted` does NOT mean an agent messaged the patient: `DraftReplyTool` is capped at **SUGGEST** and *"the agent NEVER posts"* — the flag exists only because a staff member explicitly sent it, with the HUMAN as actor (`postStaffMessage(..., aiAssisted: true)`). The portal therefore reads **"drafted with assistance, sent by the practice"** — neither a hidden machine nor an absent human (D-179). One-line revert if the mock's stance is preferred; flagged as a decision. **TWO DELIBERATE NON-ADOPTIONS:** Invoices kept its own header (its `h1` sits in a flex row beside the balance card, so the component would RESTRUCTURE rather than extract), and all three in-card empty states kept their centred treatment (wrapping them in `PortalEmptyState`, a glass-card, would nest a card inside a card). Same judgement as PT.P3's greeting: shared chrome where the shape is genuinely shared. **THE FOUR GUARDS, EACH BUILT SO IT WOULD SUCCEED WITHOUT ITS GUARD (D-182):** the unshared document is refused **404 not merely absent**; a shared document belonging to someone else is refused; the draft invoice is excluded from the list AND its PDF is unreachable; a non-participant thread is refused on read AND write; and a withdrawn consent refuses sending. **TWO GUARDS WERE INITIALLY UNPINNED AND MUTATION FOUND BOTH (D-183 — the new decision).** (1) Deleting the **ThreadParticipant membership check passed green**: `assertPatientAccess()` refuses a FOREIGN thread on the earlier `patient_id` comparison, so membership was never the deciding factor. Isolating it required the patient's **OWN** thread with their participation row `removed_at`-ed — only then is membership the last thing standing. (2) Deleting the **service's `portal.access` re-check also passed green**: over HTTP the `portal-consent` MIDDLEWARE refuses first. Proving the service re-checks required calling `postPatientMessage()` **directly**, with no middleware in front. Both layers are wanted; a test that exercises only the outer one lets the inner be deleted in silence. **Tests added:** `tests/Feature/Comms/PortalDisclosureGuardsTest.php` (7 tests, 203 assertions) — the four guards, the provenance + ceiling contract, the fence, and PT.P1's one-row-per-render (decoded, never byte-matched). **MUTATION-CHECKED SIX WAYS, all red after the two fixes:** `shared_with_patient` dropped, the documents list unscoped, draft invoices exposed, the membership check dropped, the consent re-check dropped, and provenance hidden. **THE FENCE:** no interpretation, no urgency on a message, no payment-risk framing, **no pay affordance** (D-179 — no payment path exists), and invoice rows share **ONE style** (no overdue band, D-169). The safety footnote *"for anything urgent please call the practice"* is the one place "urgent" belongs on a patient screen — excluded from the token scan for the same reason comments are (the PC.P6 lesson), with its text asserted verbatim so it cannot quietly vanish. **MY OWN PROCESS SLIP, worth recording:** I ran `migrate:fresh` for the browser check **while `composer check` was running in the background** — the exact collision I documented after PT.P1 and wrote into memory. Caught it immediately, stopped the poisoned run, did the browser work, and re-ran the check cleanly. The rule is only useful if I apply it to myself. **A LATENT FLAKE OF MY OWN, FOUND BY CI AND DIAGNOSED EMPIRICALLY (not guessed).** The full run went red on an EXISTING test I wrote at PC.P6 — `ReferralOutTest`'s fence — with `fence token 'ews' appears in the referral payload`, on a gate that touched neither that file nor its subject. It passed in isolation and as a directory. **Cause: ULIDs.** `ews` (Early Warning Score) was matched as a SUBSTRING of the payload squashed to `[a-z0-9]`, and ULIDs are Crockford base32 — an alphabet containing E, W and S. Measured over 200k ids: **0.083% of ULIDs contain 'ews'**, and with ~15 ids in a payload that is **~1.2% of runs per test**. I tested and DISCARDED two wrong hypotheses first (faker names: 0/5000; route names: none) before finding it. **Fix is PRECISION, not relaxation:** short tokens are now matched with a leading word boundary against the UNSQUASHED json — `"ews":`, `ewsScore` and `ews_score` all still fail, a ULID with 'ews' buried inside no longer does; proven with a truth table. Applied to the TWO tests that squash a PAYLOAD (`ReferralOutTest`, `NoteEditorParityTest`); the others squash file SOURCE, which carries no random ids, and were left alone. **This modifies two existing fence tests — flagged deliberately: strictly more precise, nothing weakened.** **BROWSER-VERIFIED:** the unshared "Internal note" is **absent from the list AND 404 by id**; invoices show **CHF 313.00** with **no pay button** and **ONE row style**; messages render the patient's own threads. **VERIFY:** `npm run build` green · Pint passed · PHPStan L5 "No errors" · Comms + portal suites 55 passed · full `composer check` green · `composer test:smoke` green. Docs: `PORTAL-BATCH-DIFF.md` §4d outcome + PT.P4 marked DONE (Messages adopted the shared header; Invoices and the empty states deliberately did not, and why).
- `2e593d2` PT.P5 (fifth of the PT chain): Patients/Comms/Clinical — **Portal Consents visual parity: real grants/withdrawals, stated consequences matched to the enforcing code, gating proven per type.** P0D.GU. **Opening:** top commit `ab72168` (PT.P4), tree clean, Redis PONG, CI `completed / success` confirmed BEFORE starting. **THE INVENTORY DECIDED THE GATE.** Only TWO consent scopes are enforced anywhere in CareOS: `portal.access` (portal middleware on every request · `PortalAccessService` ×3 · `ThreadService::assertPatientAccess` · `TelehealthService::joinTokenForPatient` · `DocumentService`) and `comms.email` (`NotificationService` · `SendAppointmentReminderJob` · `DraftRecallMessageTool`). Exactly two templates are seeded, `portal` and `comms`, and they map onto exactly those two scopes. **THE FEARED D-176 FAILURE IS ABSENT:** no consent on a patient's screen gates nothing. The mock's extra scopes (`documents.read`, `messages.write`, `research.share`) **do not exist in the product** — building those toggles would have been fabricating a backend to match a mock (D-170), so they were NOT built. **WHAT WAS ADDED IS THE HONEST HALF OF THE WIREFRAME — the CONSEQUENCE,** each sentence written against the enforcing code rather than the mock's prose. `portal`: *"signs you out straight away … you will not be able to sign back in, or see your documents, invoices and messages here, or join a video visit"* — every clause maps to a real check. `comms`: *"…Notices the practice must send you by law — such as an invoice reminder — are not affected."* **The carve-out IS the point:** `NotificationService` exempts the LEGAL category from the consent gate by design (D-F7), so *"we will never email you"* would have been an OVER-CLAIM. The test asserts the dunning email still sends AFTER withdrawal, which is what keeps that sentence honest — a promise the code does not keep is the same defect as a control that does nothing. **A CONSENT THE PRODUCT CANNOT DESCRIBE SAYS SO:** `copy_key` is `null` outside the described set and the page prints *"We cannot describe here exactly what withdrawing this would change. Please ask the practice."* — proven with a `research.share` template whose scope nothing enforces. **Also surfaced:** `withdrawn_at` was already in the payload and never rendered (now shown beside the grant date); `captured_by` now resolves to the staff member's NAME; and the append-only history — which the controller already listed — now says so in words. **THE GATING PROOFS, ONE PER ENFORCING LAYER (D-182/D-183):** **A1** middleware — `200` while live, `403` on the very next request after withdrawal, the consents screen included (no back door); **A2** `PortalAccessService::login()` called **DIRECTLY**, because over HTTP the middleware would refuse first and a request-level test would stay green with the login check deleted; **A3** `DocumentService::shareWithPatient()` — shares before, refused after, and the document stays unshared; **B** `NotificationService::send()` called directly with differing context so dedupe cannot be the refuser, and the positive control SENDS — which also rules out the tenant email-preference gate answering ahead of the consent gate. **MUTATION-CHECKED SEVEN WAYS, all red and each naming the intended test:** middleware check deleted · login re-check deleted · document re-check deleted · notification consent gate short-circuited · `copy_key` made unconditional · the withdraw route's ownership clause dropped · the index filtered to granted-only. **Tests added:** `tests/Feature/Patients/PortalConsentsParityTest.php` (12 tests, 101 assertions) — the multi-state fixture verified BY QUERY, the parity payload, the honest fallback, A1/A2/A3/B, the recorded withdrawal (audit JSON **decoded**, never byte-matched), the cross-patient refusal, a STRUCTURAL check that every described scope is enforced somewhere outside the tests, the fence, and PT.P1's one-row-per-render. **Helper-name collision caught before it could bite:** `ptc*` was already taken by `PortalAppointmentsParityTest`; renamed `pcn*` and verified globally unique (the PC.P3 lesson). **BROWSER-VERIFIED END TO END:** both consents render with purpose, consequence and *"Recorded by Nadia Steiner"*; withdrawing comms flips it to **Withdrawn** with both dates and keeps the record; withdrawing portal access yields the **403 "Your portal access has been withdrawn"** page on the very next request; a fresh sign-in with correct credentials is then refused **403**. Both audit rows carry `actor_type=patient` and the patient's own words as the reason. **VERIFY:** `npm run build` green · Pint passed · PHPStan L5 "No errors" (one real finding fixed on the way: `captured_by` is NOT NULL + FK-restricted, so the null branch I had written was dead code) · full `composer check` green · `composer test:smoke` green. Docs: `PORTAL-BATCH-DIFF.md` §4e outcome + PT.P5 marked DONE. Also backfilled the two stale `<pending>` markers this chain left (PT.P3 `a144fcf`, PT.P4 `ab72168`).
- `89f3fe4` PT.P6 (sixth of the PT chain): Patients/app-layer — **the portal invite LANDING PAGE** (guest surface; single-use, expiring, tenant-bound token; no enumeration; throttled; smoked). P0D.GU. **Opening:** top commit `2e593d2` (PT.P5), tree clean, Redis PONG, CI `completed / success` confirmed BEFORE starting. **WHAT WAS MISSING WAS SMALLER AND WORSE THAN "A PAGE".** The backend flow was complete — staff could invite, `PortalAccessService::acceptInvite()` could redeem — but the invitation email handed the patient a raw 64-char token and a 6-digit code **with nowhere to put them**: enrolment was reachable only by POSTing JSON. In an invite-only product with no self-registration, that means the patient-facing half of enrolment did not exist. **THE REAL CONTRACT, AS FOUND:** `invite()` REQUIRES `portal.access` consent to already exist (staff capture it), creates/reuses the `PortalAccount` in `invited` status, and stores a `PortalLoginToken` — `purpose=invite`, the token as a **SHA-256 hash** (never the token itself), a bcrypt hash of the OTP, `expires_at = now+30min`. Redemption checks hash + purpose + unconsumed + unexpired, takes the tenant **FROM the token**, verifies the OTP, re-checks consent, sets the password, flips the account `active`, stamps `consumed_at`, audits `portal.first_login` + `portal.login`. A patient needs three things: the link, the code from the same email, and a password ≥ 8. **BUILT:** `GET/POST /portal/invite/{token}` → `PortalInviteAcceptController` → `Portal/AcceptInvite.vue`, throttled **10/min** (the staff-invite ceiling, SETTINGS.P6), plus the link in `PortalInviteNotification` — the link that email always implied. Redemption still calls the SAME service method, so the account, the consent re-check, the consumption and the audit rows are the ones that already existed. No second path, no new audit action. **THE FENCE — ONE REFUSAL FOR EVERY WAY A TOKEN CAN BE DEAD:** unknown · expired · already used · bound to an account outside its own tenant → all render `{"valid": false}` and NOTHING else. No token echo, no address, no practice, no reason. Verified byte-for-byte in the browser: the only differences between two refusals are the per-session CSRF token and the URL the visitor typed themselves. **Note the near-miss:** the STAFF invite page DOES echo the token back on its invalid branch — so the mutation that adds that echo here is a plausible implementation, and it turns the suite red. **A REAL HARDENING FELL OUT OF THE TENANT-BINDING TEST.** `acceptInvite()` resolved the account with a bare `firstOrFail()`, so a token pointing at an account outside its own tenant produced a **404 while every other dead token produced a validation refusal** — a difference a prober can measure. It now raises the same "invalid invitation" refusal. That is what let the guest surface answer all four cases identically. **TWO DELIBERATE DEPARTURES FROM THE WIREFRAME, both surfaced:** (1) the mock prints *"expires 7 days after it was sent"* — **a portal invite token lives 30 MINUTES**, so the page states the row's own expiry rather than repeating a false number; (2) the mock's *"Ask the practice to resend"* is rendered as an INSTRUCTION, not a control — CareOS has no patient-triggered resend, and a button that does nothing is worse than a sentence that does (D-176). **NO CONSENT IS CAPTURED AT ENROLMENT, AND NONE WAS INVENTED (D-170).** `portal.access` is a PRECONDITION — `invite()` refuses without it, `acceptInvite()` re-checks it — and consent rows are staff-captured (`captured_by` is a NOT NULL user), so no path exists by which a patient grants one to themselves. The test asserts the consent set is identical before and after enrolment. **NO CLINICAL DATA ON THE GUEST PAGE**, and the patient's NAME is deliberately absent too: the reader already holds the email, so the address adds nothing, while a name on a token-addressed public URL is a disclosure the flow does not need. Scanned for it explicitly. **Tests added:** `tests/Feature/Patients/PortalInviteLandingTest.php` (9 tests, 92 assertions) over a FOUR-TOKEN fixture verified BY QUERY — valid · expired (perfect but for the clock) · consumed (consumed by a REAL redemption, not a hand-set column) · cross-tenant (live, unconsumed, forced in at DB level because the model's own guard refuses to create it — so the SECOND layer is what is being tested, D-183). **MUTATION-CHECKED EIGHT WAYS, all red and each naming the intended test:** single-use marker · expiry check · tenant binding on the landing page · tenant binding on redemption · a refusal that echoes the token · the throttle · the guest-smoke line — and, per the gate's own instruction, **breaking the landing page to prove the SMOKE BITES**: the AUTH-SEC.2 guest test fails and names `portal.invite.show`. **THE ONE EXISTING TEST TOUCHED — and only EXTENDED, flagged:** `RouteSmokeTest`'s guest list gains `portal.invite.show`, and a new guest-POST section smokes `portal.invite.accept` (a guest can post to it, so a GET-only smoke would leave the half that actually provisions an account uncovered). Strictly stricter; nothing relaxed. **BROWSER-VERIFIED END TO END:** the valid link renders practice + invited address + the REAL expiry; enrolment completes and lands on `/portal`; the patient then signs in at `/portal/login` with the password they chose; **replaying the same link shows the generic refusal**; expired and unknown are identical; the throttle engages (200×8 then 429). Audit shows exactly `portal.first_login` + `portal.login` for the enrolment, and the consents unchanged. **VERIFY:** `npm run build` green · Pint passed · PHPStan L5 "No errors" (one real finding fixed: `portal_accounts.email` is NOT NULL, so the null branch I wrote was dead) · full `composer check` green · `composer test:smoke` green including the new guest lines. Docs: `PORTAL-BATCH-DIFF.md` §4f + PT.P6 marked DONE + **the inventory error corrected in all three places** (screen 9 mapped "Invite" to `Auth/AcceptInvite.vue`, which is the STAFF invite — a different flow).
- `37fe379` PT.P6-FIX (follow-up to PT.P6 `89f3fe4`, which went **RED ON CI** while green locally — the SECOND time this exact class of divergence has bitten this chain, after PT.P1-FIX). **THE FAILURE:** two of PT.P6's own new tests — `NO ENUMERATION` and `THROTTLED` — failed on CI with `429` where `200` was expected. Nothing about the feature was wrong; the TESTS were environment-dependent. **THE CAUSE, diagnosed and then REPRODUCED locally before fixing anything:** `phpunit.xml` sets `CACHE_STORE=array`, but a **non-forced `<env>` does not override a real environment variable** — and `.github/workflows` exports `CACHE_STORE: redis`. So locally the rate-limiter bucket is an array store recreated for every test, while on CI it is one long-lived Redis key that SURVIVES between tests. A file making a dozen requests to a `throttle:10,1` route therefore poisons its own later tests — on CI only. **Worse, and worth remembering:** Laravel's guest throttle signature is `sha1(domain|ip)` — **not the path** — so EVERY `throttle:10,1` guest route shares ONE bucket per visitor. The staff-invite requests in another test count against the portal-invite budget. **PROVEN, not guessed:** re-running the file locally with `CACHE_STORE=redis REDIS_CLIENT=predis` reproduced **exactly** the two CI failures, in the same order. With the fix, the same command is green — and so is the ordinary local run. **THE FIX:** a `beforeEach()` in the new test file emptying the limiter store (`Cache::store(config('cache.limiter'))->flush()` — `cache.limiter` is null-by-default and falls through to the default store, which is the supported handle Laravel's own RateLimiter uses). Every test now starts from a KNOWN bucket. **NOTHING WEAKENED:** the throttle is still asserted (10 × 200 then 429), and the mutation re-check confirms it — deleting `throttle:10,1` from the routes still turns the suite red. The fix changes the starting point, not the assertion. **THE LESSON, generalised past this gate:** a test that depends on a CACHE or QUEUE store is environment-dependent in a way a DB test is not — `RefreshDatabase` resets the database between tests and **nothing resets the cache**. Any test asserting on a rate limit, a lock, a dedupe key or a cached value must either clear its own store first or assert something order-independent. And the general local-vs-CI rule now has a second instance: **check whether phpunit's `<env>` is actually winning**, because a real env var beats it silently. **VERIFY:** file green under BOTH cache stores · Pint passed · PHPStan L5 "No errors" · full `composer check` green · `composer test:smoke` green.
- `ae00b5a` PT.P7 (seventh and FINAL core gate of the PT chain — **the PORTAL BATCH CORE IS NOW COMPLETE**): Patients — **the portal password-reset broker.** P0D.GU. **Opening:** top commit `37fe379` (PT.P6-FIX), tree clean, Redis PONG, CI `completed / success` confirmed BEFORE starting. **THE GAP WAS TOTAL:** Fortify's reset broker runs over `users`, portal accounts live in `portal_accounts`, and nothing bridged them — a patient who forgot their password had exactly one option, telephone the practice and ask to be re-invited. **REUSED RATHER THAN REBUILT (the gate's first requirement).** `PortalLoginToken` already had every property needed and PT.P6 had just proven all of them: SHA-256 hash instead of the token, bcrypt OTP, expiry, `consumed_at` single-use marker, tenant taken FROM the row. So the broker is **a new `purpose` on the same table** — `password_reset` — and the single lookup that decides whether a token is alive (`redeemableToken`) now takes the purpose as part of the question. A second token table would have meant a second set of these properties to get right AND to keep right. **The purpose is what makes the sharing safe**, and it is proven in BOTH directions: an invite token on the reset route and a reset token on the invite route are refused exactly like tokens that never existed. **NO ENUMERATION, tested against FOUR genuinely different subjects** — a live account, an unknown address, a real patient who was never invited, and a DISABLED account — plus a fifth in another tenant. One response, one redirect, one page. **The positive control is the point:** the answers are identical WHILE exactly one subject was actually emailed, which is the difference they conceal. The mutation adding *"we couldn't find that address"* turns the suite red. (The gate asked for "another tenant with the SAME email if the schema allows"; it does not — `portal_accounts.email` is globally unique, and a test now PINS that fact rather than leaving the choice to a comment.) **A RESET CHANGES EXACTLY ONE THING.** It does not revive a disabled account (no token is issued at all), does not touch consent, and **does not sign anybody in** — the patient lands on the sign-in page and goes through the real `login()`. **Browser-proven end to end:** a patient whose `portal.access` was withdrawn completed a reset (password genuinely changed, verified in the DB) and was then **refused 403 at sign-in**. Recovery is not a way around the gate PT.P5 built. Pinned at the SERVICE, called directly, because over HTTP the middleware answers first (D-183). **TOKEN HYGIENE:** only the hash is stored — asserted COLUMN BY COLUMN against the raw token, not just on `token_hash` — the OTP is bcrypt, and a new request **SUPERSEDES** the account's older live reset links (stamping the existing `consumed_at`, so there is ONE rule for what makes a token dead, not two). The invite flow keeps its own behaviour; not this gate's to change. **TWO WIREFRAME DEPARTURES, both deliberate:** the mock's password **strength meter** ("Strong") is not built — the server's rule is a minimum length and nothing else, so a verdict the code cannot back is a judgment invented for the screen (D-176); and **"Resend available in 0:47"** is not built — there is no per-address cooldown to count down to, only the route throttle. **ONE WIREFRAME PROMISE NOT BUILT AND FLAGGED RATHER THAN FAKED:** *"a successful reset invalidates every other session server-side"*. Portal sessions are not tracked per account (no `AuthenticateSession` in the portal stack, no session index), so CareOS cannot do this today — **so the page does not claim it** (D-179). It is a real security improvement and the right follow-up, but it needs the portal middleware stack changed. **Tests added:** `tests/Feature/Patients/PortalPasswordResetTest.php` (14 tests, 188 assertions) — the four-subject fixture verified BY QUERY, no-enumeration, the reset through the existing path (one audit row on `portal.password_reset`, `consumed_at` stamped, NOT signed in), token hygiene + supersede, single-use, expiry, purpose scoping both ways, tenant binding (two tests), a reset grants nothing, a reset revives nothing, the throttle, the smoke coverage, and the fence. **MUTATION-CHECKED TWELVE WAYS, all red, each naming the intended test** — including the two proving the guest smoke BITES (breaking the reset page names `portal.password.reset`; breaking the request form names `portal.password.send`). **A DEFECT IN MY OWN TEST, CAUGHT BY MUTATION — the PT.P4 lesson repeating.** The first tenant-binding test asserted that a beta token resolves as beta even when the session says alpha; an UNSCOPED account lookup passed it, because an account id is globally unique and resolves either way. The case it missed is the one where the token's tenant and its account's tenant DISAGREE. A second test now forces exactly that row, and the mutation goes red. **A RELATED TRAP worth remembering:** wrapping a query in `TenantContext::system()` while a tenant is IN CONTEXT is a **no-op** — `TenantScope` checks `has()` FIRST — so "mutate the lookup to system mode" does not test tenant binding at all. I chased that for two runs before probing the branch instead of guessing; the real mutation is to change where the TENANT comes from (session instead of token), and that one turns red immediately. **THE ONE EXISTING TEST TOUCHED — EXTENDED ONLY, flagged:** `RouteSmokeTest`'s guest list gains `portal.password.request` + `portal.password.reset` (GET) and `portal.password.update` (POST), and the redirecting request form is smoked separately (it returns 302, so it cannot join the 200 list). Strictly stricter. **A near-miss of my own making:** the first smoke patch anchored on `expect(implode(...))`, which appears in FOUR tests in that file, and inserted itself into all of them. Caught immediately by counting the insertions; the harness now REFUSES to apply an anchor that is not unique. **VERIFY:** `npm run build` green · Pint passed · PHPStan L5 "No errors" · full `composer check` green · `composer test:smoke` green including the new guest lines. **BROWSER-VERIFIED:** the request form reaches "Check your email" with the conditional wording; the emailed link opens the set-password page (practice name, real expiry, no address, no name); the reset completes and the new password signs in; **the replayed link shows the generic refusal, byte-identical to an unknown token**; the throttle goes 200×10 then 429 from a clean bucket; and the withdrawn-consent 403 above. - `ec54c5b` GOV.P4 (first of the GOV chain — the governance & AI batch audit is `65bee32`): seeders/tests only — **demo governance data via the REAL approve/reject/fence paths.** P0D.GU. **Opening:** top commit `65bee32`, tree clean, Redis PONG, CI `completed / success` confirmed BEFORE starting. **THE GAP:** the demo seed produced `pending` and `fence_refused` only, so the Resolved and Rejected governance screens had nothing to show and AGENT.P5's approved-as-is % correctly rendered "—" (nothing had ever resolved). **EVERY STATE IS NOW REACHED BY DRIVING ITS REAL PATH — no status column written, no ledger row inserted:** executed-AS-IS = `propose(scheduler.suggest_slots)` → `ApprovalQueue::approve()` by reception (`appointment.manage`); executed-EDITED = `propose(comms.draft_reply)` → `approve($action, $reviewer, $editedPayload)` by reception (`comms.manage`); rejected = `propose(clinical.draft_recall_message)` → `reject()` with its required reason by Dr Brunner (`note.write`); fence_refused = unchanged (propose a non-groundable draft, approve, the fence fires). **A TOOL-SAFETY MISTAKE THAT AN EXISTING INVARIANT CAUGHT.** I first used `billing.preflight_invoice` for the approved-as-is action, reasoning that a "preflight report" writes nothing. **It writes:** its charge validator PERSISTS validation state and flipped the demo's DUNNING-FEE charge from `draft` to `validated`, breaking an assertion an earlier gate had pinned. I did NOT touch that assertion — I changed the tool, to `scheduler.suggest_slots`, whose execute only reads the availability finder and whose own result says `books_on_approval: false`. **The lesson: a "report" can still write; read the execute path, and let the pinned invariants tell you when you were wrong.** **THE TIME SPREAD, and a conclusion I reached backwards and had to correct.** I reasoned that back-dating was IMPOSSIBLE: `verifyChain()` replays the hash chain in `occurred_at ASC` order, so a back-dated audit row would sort before its predecessor and break `prev_hash`. I was ready to write that up as the gate's reconciliation. **I tested it instead of asserting it — and it was wrong.** `AuditService::record()` forces `occurred_at` strictly MONOTONIC per tenant (`prevTime + 1µs` when the clock is not ahead), precisely so the stored order and the verification order always agree. So travelling the clock is safe by construction. The spread is therefore real: each call runs inside `Carbon::setTestNow` (always restored in a `finally`), so the action AND its append-only ledger rows move together — rejected 12 days ago, executed-as-is 5 days ago, edited-then-approved 2 days ago, the fence refusal + both pending proposals today. Seeder + tests only. **VERIFY:** Pint passed · PHPStan L5 "No errors" · full `composer check` green · `composer test:smoke` green.
