- `8f0b2e8` GOV.P5 (fifth and FINAL gate of the GOV chain — **the GOVERNANCE & AI batch core is now COMPLETE**): app-layer/Platform — **the governance-ledger exporter.** P0D.GU. **Opening:** top commit `57afa3f` (GOV.P3), tree clean, CI `completed / success` confirmed BEFORE starting. **REDIS WAS DOWN and is not installed on this machine** — it does not affect the suite (phpunit uses array cache/session/queue) and nothing in the exporter touches it; the browser check ran with a file cache. **AND A CORRECTION:** I reported "Redis PONG" in the GOV.P3 gate report when that probe had printed NOTHING — the claim was unverified. Nothing in GOV.P3 depended on Redis, but the statement should not have been made. **THE COLUMN-BY-COLUMN PHI CLASSIFICATION (the gate's core decision, made before any code).** IN by default: `id` · `occurred_at` · `agent`(+resolved name) · `feature` · `outcome` · `tool` (lifted from `tool_calls[0]`, a registry key) · `provider`/`model`/`model_version` · `prompt_hash` (a hash, not a prompt) · the token/cost/latency counters · `approver_id` (a STAFF id — an accountability record with no actor is not an audit trail) · `output_ref` (a pointer, no content). OUT by default: `metadata` (caller-supplied JSON; the demo's own rows carry staff prose and nothing stops a future caller putting a patient's words there) · `error_message` (provider errors echo payloads) · raw `tool_calls`. NEVER: `tenant_id` · `label` · `created_at`/`updated_at`. **The rule for an unclear column is OUT**, because a file that leaves the building cannot be recalled. **`audit_events` IS NOT EXPORTED ROW-BY-ROW AT ALL** — it carries `patient_id` on most rows and a free-text `reason` that in this very demo reads *"Patient ist erkrankt"* and *"Weight was measured during the consultation"*: clinical content in a column no filter could sanitise reliably. What the export takes from it is its INTEGRITY STATE (chain verdict + event count) in the manifest — the guarantee without the content. **MINIMISATION, proven the only way it can be:** the test first asserts the SOURCE rows genuinely contain the prose, then asserts the default file does not, and that no patient id appears. The mutation making free text default turns it red. **INTEGRITY:** every export ships a manifest — no option to omit it, and the download is a ZIP of payload + manifest so the two cannot be separated by accident. It records window · filters · row count · actor · moment · opt-ins · a SHA-256 of the exact payload bytes · byte length · chain state. Truncation (drop the last row) and alteration (change one word) are both proven detectable. **SELF-AUDIT:** one row on the EXISTING path (`governance.ledger_exported`) with actor, window, filters, row count, opt-ins and the payload hash. It is written AFTER the snapshot, so it cannot be inside its own export — **and the manifest SAYS SO** rather than leaving a reader to wonder (D-179). **SCOPE:** a NEW permission **`audit.export`**, added the ARDETAIL.P6 way rather than overloading `audit.view` — three roles may read the log on screen, ONE may take it out as a file. Both halves asserted: the records officer opens the dashboard and is refused the export. Tenant isolation pinned AT THE EXPORTER (called directly, D-183) against a second tenant's row that sits inside the window with a distinctive marker, **with the free-text opt-in ON** — the widest the export can ever be. **NO MUTATION:** the ledger row count is unchanged and `verifyChain()` still passes AFTER the export's own audit row is appended. **OMITTED AND STATED:** the wireframe's SIGNED PDF is not built — there is no signing key in this build, and a "signed" file with an unmanaged key is worse than an unsigned one because it invites trust it cannot support. The export ships a checksum manifest instead, and the dashboard's omission card names it with that reason, asserted as RENDERED (GOV.P3's lesson). **Tests added:** `tests/Feature/Governance/LedgerExportTest.php` (11 tests, ~100 assertions). **MUTATION-CHECKED EIGHT WAYS.** Red: free text exported by default · the manifest hash a constant · the export not audited · the gate dropped to `audit.view` · the exporter ignoring the tenant scope · the PHI opt-in pre-ticked · (after the fix) the free-text gate deleted. **A GAP MUTATION FOUND — the free-text opt-in's OWN gate was unpinned.** Deleting it left the suite green, because the only SEEDED role holding `audit.export` (org_admin) also holds `admin.manage`: the outer gate always answered first. A new test builds the user the catalogue does not — a role with `audit.export` and nothing else — who CAN export and IS refused the free text. Same shape as PT.P7's tenant binding and GOV.P2's `status = OPEN` conjunct: **a guard behind another guard needs a subject only IT can refuse.** **AND ONE MUTATION THAT WAS A NO-OP (D-187), recorded rather than counted as a catch:** making the value-writer ignore the free-text flag changes nothing, because the COLUMN LIST is what gates it — the writer is never called for a column that is not in the list. Proven before concluding anything. **A PROCESS MISTAKE I REPEATED:** I reseeded the dev database and attempted logins WHILE `composer check` was running, which produced two red tests that were pure interference (a migration collision, and two extra `auth.login_failed` audit rows counted by an existing test). Both pass in isolation; the check was re-run cleanly. This is the documented shared-database collision — I have now hit it twice in this engagement. **VERIFY:** `npm run build` green · Pint passed · PHPStan L5 "No errors" (one real finding fixed: a redundant `??` on a non-nullable verifyChain key) · full `composer check` green on the clean re-run · `composer test:smoke` green. **No existing behaviour test modified.** Docs: `GOVERNANCE-AI-BATCH-DIFF.md` §13 with the full classification table + G3 and the GOV.P5 chain row marked DONE + **the batch core marked COMPLETE**.
- `3099ac5` TASK — COMMS BATCH (fifth domain batch; **audit + report ONLY, no app code, nothing fixed**): decode + parity-audit all 8 Comms wireframes. **Opening:** top commit `8f0b2e8` (GOV.P5), tree clean, CI `completed / success` from the check-run API. **REDIS: PROBED AND NOT REACHABLE** on this machine (connection refused; not installed) — stated rather than assumed, and nothing in an audit needs it. **THE HEADLINE FINDING: three of the eight screens are built on a consent model the product does not have.** CareOS enforces exactly ONE outbound-comms consent — `comms.email`, per patient, all-or-nothing for non-legal mail, with the LEGAL carve-out never consent-gated (D-F7/D-184). The mocks assume per-TOPIC consent ("Health campaigns & marketing", "Recalls & check-ups"), per-CHANNEL consent (portal · email · SMS), a client/HOUSEHOLD grouping ("Keller household · CL-2024-0192 · 3 patients") and a CAMPAIGN/outreach feature. **VERIFIED BY QUERY: none of the four exists** — two consent templates (`portal` → `portal.access`, `comms` → `comms.email`), and no household, client or campaign table anywhere. This is the Comms analogue of GOV.P1's nine invented acting tool keys: not a little ahead of the backend, a different product. **CHANNEL HONESTY:** three channel CONSTANTS are declared (`email`, `sms`, `portal`) but **only `EmailNotificationDriver` is wired** — an SMS template fails closed TWICE (no `sms` key in `CONSENT_SCOPES` → `no_consent`; no driver → `channel_unavailable`), and `NotificationPreferenceService`'s own docblock says "there is no SMS preference because no SMS channel is wired". Drawing an SMS toggle even switched OFF asserts the channel exists and is merely disabled — D-176 in its purest form. **PREFERENCES ARE TENANT-LEVEL, NOT PATIENT-LEVEL:** `MANAGEABLE = [appointment.reminder, waitlist.offer, telehealth.invite]` is an ADMIN toggle for the whole tenant. There is no per-patient topic switch, and the portal offers WITHDRAWAL only — no self-service opt-in (PT.P5). **TWO MORE REFUSALS on the reminder-sent screen:** a **`delivered`** state (`NotificationDelivery` records `queued`/`sent`/`failed`/`skipped` — `sent` means handed to the mailer, nothing records a receipt) and a **17-second UNDO/recall** (there is no unsend; PT.P6 refused the identical 18-second affordance for the same reason). **FENCE VERIFICATION — the live build already enforces more than the mocks show:** consent is checked in `NotificationService::send()` with the TEMPLATE owning the category (a caller cannot relabel marketing as legal); a non-send is a RECORDED row with a `skipped_reason`, not silence; thread visibility is MEMBERSHIP + active portal account + `portal.access`, each layer pinned separately (PT.P4); the agent drafts and a HUMAN sends via `ApprovalQueue::approve()` (re-authorise + re-ground + `ai_assisted`), and `comms.draft_reply` is ceiling-clamped at SUGGEST twice; a clinical question produces **NO draft** (`InboxAgent::refuseClinical`); telehealth has **no recording affordance anywhere** and the join token is issued on demand and never stored; `DunningEvent` and `TelehealthParticipant` are append-only at model AND DB-trigger level. **FIXTURE VERIFIED BY QUERY (not assumed):** threads 5 patient (1 flagged `clinician_attention_at`) + 1 internal · participants 7 staff / 5 patient · messages 5 patient / 3 staff, **1 staff `ai_assisted=1`** (agent-drafted, human-sent) · `comms.email` granted for 7 of 15 patients, so both with- and without-consent exist · deliveries **1 sent + 1 skipped/`no_consent`** — so "was it actually sent" has a real answer on the demo data. **PROPOSED CHAIN: 2 core + 2 optional.** COMMS.P1 Unified Inbox parity (chrome + the patient context pane) · COMMS.P2 Telehealth Sessions + Join parity (pre-join device check client-side; waiting/elapsed from `TelehealthParticipant.joined_at`) · optional COMMS.P3 an HONEST "reminder sent" confirmation · optional COMMS.P4 a notification feed. **AND ONE RECOMMENDATION TO DECLINE:** screens 2, 4 and 6 (Consent-Blocked Draft · Opt-in Confirmed · Request Consent Update) **should not enter a parity chain at all** — they are not a gap between wireframe and build, they are a different consent architecture plus a marketing capability the product has deliberately not built. A reduced version would be WORSE than omission: a topic list that only ever holds one topic, or a channel matrix with one live column, teaches the user something false about what the practice can promise a patient. Docs: `docs/wireframe-parity/COMMS-BATCH-DIFF.md` (8 screens, the real-machinery map, the does-not-exist table, per-screen deltas, all six fence areas, shared components + backend gaps C1–C5, the proposed chain). **Nothing fixed, no app code, no tests, no seeders touched; `resources/prototype/` stays gitignored.**
- `11cf333` COMMS.P1 (first of the COMMS chain): Comms — **unified inbox visual parity + the patient context pane.** P0D.GU. **Opening:** top commit `9942063` (the comms audit follow-up, later than the expected `3099ac5`), tree clean, CI `completed / success` from the check-run API. **REDIS PROBED AND NOT REACHABLE** (connection refused; not installed on this box) — the suite uses array drivers and nothing here touches it. Fixture reseeded and VERIFIED BY QUERY before starting: 5 patient threads (1 flagged `clinician_attention_at`, open) + 1 internal · 5 patient / 3 staff messages, **1 staff `ai_assisted=1` sent by Nadia Steiner** · comms.email granted for 7 of 15 patients · deliveries 1 sent + 1 skipped/`no_consent`. **WHAT WAS ALREADY RIGHT AND WAS LEFT ALONE:** the dark thread header, filter pills, derived unread, internal chip, clinician-attention banner, the dashed agent-draft card with source chips, and the ABSENCE of a request-draft button on a flagged thread. This gate added a context pane, a filter, two counts, a provenance line and an omission card, and **changed no rule**. **THE CONTEXT PANE — FIVE ELEMENTS, FIVE SEPARATE GATES, enforced in the READER (not the controller, not the page):** identity → `patient.view` · **recorded allergies → `encounter.manage`** · next appointment → `appointment.manage` · open balance → `billing.view` via `PatientBalanceReader::present()` (no page arithmetic) · email-contact status → the inbox's own `comms.manage`. **THE ALLERGY GATE IS THE ONE JUDGMENT CALL:** the CHART shows allergies at `patient.view`, but the chart is a clinical surface opened deliberately and the inbox is an operational one reception works in. Where the right gate is arguable, GOV.P5's rule applies — take the STRICTER one, because a disclosure cannot be recalled. **REFUSED MEANS EMPTY, NOT A PLAUSIBLE DEFAULT.** A `0.00` balance or an empty allergy list would tell the viewer something false — "none recorded" and "you may not look" are different claims and the pane renders them differently. Pinned with a user the CATALOGUE DOES NOT BUILD: four users each holding `comms.manage` + exactly ONE element permission, so each element's own gate is the only thing that can refuse it (the seeded reception role bundles `comms.manage` WITH `patient.view`, which would have hidden the identity gate entirely — the GOV.P5 / PT.P7 shape). **NO JUDGMENT ANYWHERE:** no diagnosis, acuity, risk, triage, priority, SLA or computed summary; recorded severity travels as the clinician's own word and allergies are ordered BY SUBSTANCE (D-169/D-173). **ONE DEFINITION OF "STILL NEEDS A HUMAN":** the GOV.P2 conjunction (flagged AND still open AND no staff message since the flag) MOVED onto the model as `Thread::scopeWaitingForClinician()`, with `NeedsHumanReader` delegating to it — so the inbox filter, the inbox count and the governance dashboard cannot describe different sets. Asserted that the two surfaces agree. The conjunction is unchanged and GOV.P2's suite is green WITHOUT modification. **PROVENANCE — BOTH FACTS TOGETHER:** an agent-drafted message shows that a draft was involved AND names the human whose Send posted it. Not an inference: `DraftReplyTool::execute()` passes the HUMAN as actor, so `author_staff_user_id` genuinely IS the sender. PT.P4 left the PORTAL-side wording an open decision; this is the staff side, where naming the colleague is the accountability record. **THE DECLINED AFFORDANCES, STATED ON THE PAGE** (a "what this screen does not show" card, asserted as RENDERED via the keys the component iterates — GOV.P3's lesson): no SMS/phone/WhatsApp (one driver is wired; a greyed second column would assert the channel exists — D-176) · no "delivered"/"read" (the record says handed-to-the-mailer; nothing reports back) · no undo/recall (PT.P6 refused the identical affordance) · no per-topic/per-channel consent switches (D-188). **A RECONCILIATION THE GATE'S OWN WORDING ASSUMED OTHERWISE, surfaced rather than papered over:** the gate asked for "a reply refused at the SERVICE with a recorded reason row" — but **A THREAD REPLY DOES NOT EMAIL ANYONE.** `ThreadService` never calls `NotificationService`; a staff reply is an in-portal message read behind the three-layer portal check. Building an email-on-reply would have been exactly the NEW SEND PATH the gate's own rules forbid. So the consent refusal is pinned where it actually lives — `NotificationService::send()` returning `skipped`/`no_consent`, called DIRECTLY (D-183), with the positive control that granting consent makes the identical call SEND. The fixture patient needed a real email contact row first, or the send would have skipped for `no_recipient_address` BEFORE consent could be the deciding factor (D-182). **THE THREE PATIENT-SIDE VISIBILITY LAYERS RE-PINNED SEPARATELY** (membership · active portal account · `portal.access`), each with the other two left satisfied and a positive control that all three together permit the read. **Tests added:** `tests/Feature/Comms/InboxParityTest.php` (18 tests, 946 assertions). **MUTATION-CHECKED TWELVE WAYS.** Ten caught first time: allergy gate deleted · balance gate deleted · a refused element returning `0.00` · a computed risk score added · the raw flag replacing the conjunction · the "no staff reply" conjunct inverted · provenance dropping the sender · an SMS symbol on the page · the omission loop emptied · the consent copy over-claiming "we will never email you". **TWO ESCAPED, AND BOTH WERE THE SAME DEFECT IN MY FIXTURE RATHER THAN IN THE CODE — D-174 in its exact form.** (1) Ordering by SEVERITY stayed green because the fixture recorded Aspirin=mild and Penicillin=severe, so substance-order and severity-order produced the SAME LIST; Aspirin is now recorded `unknown`, which sorts after `severe` alphabetically and before it by any clinical reading, so the two orderings genuinely disagree. (2) Counting the CAPPED PAGE instead of the record stayed green because the fixture held ONE thread, so list and count were both 1; a second unflagged thread now makes the list longer than the count. **A test whose fixture makes two different implementations agree is not testing the difference.** **VERIFY:** `npm run build` green · `vue-tsc` clean for `Comms/Inbox.vue` (the project has pre-existing type errors elsewhere — this is why GOV.P5's missing `ref` import reached the browser) · Pint passed · PHPStan L5 "No errors" (one real finding fixed: a nullsafe call on a non-nullable `date_of_birth`) · full `composer check` green · `composer test:smoke` green. **No existing behaviour test modified.** Docs: `COMMS-BATCH-DIFF.md` §14 + the COMMS.P1 chain row and the C1 gap marked DONE.
- `ab9e62c` COMMS.P2 (second and FINAL core gate of the COMMS chain — **the COMMS batch core is now COMPLETE**): Comms — **telehealth sessions + join parity.** P0D.GU. **Opening:** top commit `11cf333` (COMMS.P1), tree clean, CI `completed / success` from the check-run API. **REDIS PROBED AND NOT REACHABLE** (not installed); the suite uses array drivers. **THE FINDING THAT SHAPED THE GATE — WHAT PRESENCE THE BACKEND CAN ACTUALLY REPORT.** Recorded: session created · token ISSUED (an audit row) · participant JOINED (`joined_at`) · LEFT (`left_at`, settable once) · `started_at` / `ended_at`. **NOT recorded: whether anyone is connected right now.** `left_at` is only written when something calls `recordLeave()`, so a dropped connection leaves it null forever — "joined and has not left" is NOT "is in the call". That single fact disposes of the wireframe's most prominent features: the pulsing "patient waiting" ring, the **10-minute wait threshold**, the amber escalation and "Notify running late" all rest on live presence. REFUSED and STATED on screen. The threshold was doubly wrong — an escalation band is also a computed urgency grading (D-169). **AND A TOKEN IS NOT A JOIN (D-179):** issuing one means somebody asked to join, not that they did; the reader counts only participant rows. **A REAL GAP FOUND, REPORTED, AND DELIBERATELY NOT "FIXED": no HTTP path calls `recordJoin()`/`recordLeave()`** — both are exercised only by tests and the new demo seeding, so in the live app the participant table is never written. The tempting fix (call `recordJoin()` when the token endpoint fires) was REJECTED: a token is issued BEFORE anyone connects, so recording a join there would assert an action that may never be taken (D-179). Doing it honestly needs the client to report back that it connected — a new capability, outside a parity gate. The surface shows what IS recorded, says plainly what is not, and the gap is written down rather than papered over. **THE LIST:** **ENDED sessions are now visible** — the previous controller filtered to created/active only, so a clinician could see the future and never the past. Each row carries the linked appointment's real time, the recorded created/started/ended stamps, and the RECORDED JOINS with their times. The state (scheduled / joined / ended) is **DERIVED** from the recorded status plus the participant rows, never read from one column something could have written directly. Filters over those real attributes; `StatCard` counts over the whole record (D-166/D-174); honest empty state; a clinician with no sessions sees an empty list, never someone else's. **THE PRE-JOIN SURFACE** (new READ-ONLY route `GET /telehealth/{session}`, `encounter.manage`-gated, tenant-scoped): **the device check is entirely LOCAL** — `getUserMedia` in the browser, tracks stopped immediately, result shown to the clinician and nobody else: never posted, never recorded, never sent with the join. It reports **availability only** — no bandwidth, no signal bars, no quality grade; we can honestly say a camera was found, we cannot score a call that has not happened. **IT GATES NOTHING:** a request claiming `precheck: passed` and one claiming `failed` produce identical server behaviour because neither is read — asserted directly, so a forged claim is worthless by construction. **AN UNCONFIGURED PROVIDER IS STATED UP FRONT:** the deploy template ships `livekit.invalid` with no credentials and `LiveKitProvider` throws on an empty secret, so every Join would fail at the moment of use; the surface says so and WITHHOLDS the control instead of offering something that cannot work (D-176). **THE OMISSIONS, each stated on screen and asserted as RENDERED** (GOV.P3): recording in any form · transcript / AI summary · connection-quality score · live-presence indicator and wait-time alert. On recording specifically: rooms are created with `recording_disabled` (the provider REFUSES otherwise) and every token withholds `roomRecord`/`roomAdmin`/`recorder` — nothing to start, stop or list, and deliberately **no disabled button**, which would tell a reader the capability exists. **THE DEMO FIXTURE, DRIVEN THROUGH THE REAL PATH:** `DemoClinicSeeder::seedTelehealth()` opens three sessions and reaches each state by TRAVERSING the service — `createSessionFromAppointment()`, `recordJoin()` (which is what flips a session to ACTIVE and stamps `started_at`), `recordLeave()`, `endSession()`. The PROVIDER, not the service, is swapped for the in-memory adapter — exactly what the swappable-adapter design (D-G1) exists for. Verified by query: created/0 legs · active/1 leg (staff joined, patient has NOT) · ended/2 legs both left; no token column in either table; real `telehealth.session_created/started/ended` audit rows. **Tests added:** `tests/Feature/Comms/TelehealthSessionsParityTest.php` (11 tests, 1413 assertions). **MUTATION-CHECKED TWELVE WAYS.** Eleven caught first time: the token persisted into the audit context · a fabricated `liveConnected` derived from a null `left_at` · a quality score added · the state faked instead of derived · the append-only participant guard removed · the server trusting a client pre-check claim · the pre-join permission gate dropped · the omission loop emptied · a recording control added to the component · an unconfigured provider reported as configured · the token endpoint un-scoped from its tenant. **ONE ESCAPED — D-189 FOR THE THIRD TIME:** hardcoding the counts as `[1, 1, 1]` stayed green because the fixture held exactly one session in each state, so the correct implementation and the constant agreed. A fourth session makes the distribution asymmetric. The pattern is now frequent enough to check by default: **when a test pins a derivation, the fixture must be lopsided enough that a constant cannot impersonate it.** **A PHPStan finding fixed properly rather than suppressed:** two closures had unresolvable generic types over a `groupBy` collection; the participants are now presented up front into a plain `array<string, list<array>>` — no inline `@var` override, which the project forbids. **VERIFY:** `npm run build` green · `vue-tsc` clean for both Telehealth pages · Pint passed · PHPStan L5 "No errors" · full `composer check` green · `composer test:smoke` green. **No existing behaviour test modified.** Docs: `COMMS-BATCH-DIFF.md` §15 + the COMMS.P2 chain row and C2 marked done + **the batch core marked COMPLETE with screens 2/4/6 declined under D-188**.
- `7390006` TASK — SCHEDULING BATCH (sixth and FINAL buildable domain batch; **audit + report ONLY, no app code, nothing fixed**): decode + parity-audit all 8 Scheduling wireframes. **Opening:** top commit `ab9e62c` (COMMS.P2), tree clean, CI `completed / success` from the check-run API. **REDIS PROBED AND NOT REACHABLE** (not installed). **COMMS.P2's `<pending>` LOG hash backfilled to `ab9e62c`** as this task's carried-forward housekeeping. **THE HEADLINE: scheduling is the STRONGEST backend in the product, so the gap is not capability — it is JUDGMENT.** One locked booking path, a closed transition machine, real availability templates WITH dated exceptions, a real waitlist with offers/TTL/expiry. Three screens add a ranking, a triage or an auto-send the fences forbid; two invent a PRICE on the service catalog that lives in Billing. **FIXTURE COMPLETED BY DRIVING THE REAL SERVICES** in a throwaway scratchpad script (never committed, no app code) because the demo seeder alone was not asymmetric enough: appointments across ALL EIGHT statuses (booked 5, rest 1) · resource loads 6/5/4/4/0 · **a SOFT-SUSPENDED branch (`accepts_online_bookings=0`) alongside a bookable one** · waitlist priorities 0/2/5/10 · offers in offered/declined/expired · real `waitlist_offer.*` audit rows. **FENCE 1 — NO OPTIMISATION.** REFUSED: the Day-Board's **ranked conflict resolver** (three moves, one badged "best") — nothing ranks moves today, `checkAvailability()` answers free/conflict for ONE slot a human picked; the waitlist's **"routine offers can go automatically at Level 1"** (the tool is ceilinged at **APPROVE**, already refused in Dental); auto-re-offer after expiry (`expireDue()` expires, nothing re-offers). KEPT: acceptance seats the chair through `BookingService`, never page-side. **FENCE 2 — NO PREDICTION.** REFUSED: no-show **risk triage** (screen 8's whole premise); waitlist **"Urgent · clinical"**. KEPT/ACCEPTED: the waiting-room elapsed time (real `checked_in_at` minus real `starts_at`), the "cancellation freed 30 min" figure, and **per-lane utilisation as a plain untinted ratio** — explicitly allowed by the gate, provided it stays unranked (D-169). TRIMMED: "on schedule" (a judgment) and availability's "worth holding urgent capacity" (advice, not a recorded fact). **A FENCE HOLE FOUND IN THE BACKEND ITSELF:** `WaitlistEntry.priority` is a bare undocumented `int` that `matchingForSlot()` orders by FIRST. Nothing states it is operational and nothing stops a UI labelling it clinical — **the moment a screen says "Urgent · clinical", a clinical judgment enters a scheduling ranking.** Recommended: any waitlist gate must give `priority` a WRITTEN operational meaning before building a UI over it. **FENCE 3 — THE CONCURRENCY GUARD.** Every write path (staff book · `bookOnline` · quick-book · series · reschedule · **waitlist accept**) funnels through `createBooking()` → `lockResource()` (SELECT … FOR UPDATE) → `assertNoOverlap()` widened by the SERVICE's buffers. REFUSED: **"Keep both (override)"** — there is no override parameter and `assertNoOverlap` throws; the mock's own caption ("availability re-checked server-side") contradicts the button beside it. **AND AN OVER-CLAIM FOUND: the waitlist "slot held until 18:00 … no one loses the slot to silence" IS NOT TRUE** — the hold is ONE OPEN OFFER PER ENTRY (`OPEN_STATUSES`, used only to block double-offering the same patient); `assertNoOverlap` never reads `waitlist_offers`, so reception or a public booker can take the slot while an offer is outstanding and the acceptance then fails. Either reword to "we hold your place in the queue, not the slot" or build a real reservation. **FENCE 4 — LEGAL TRANSITIONS.** `booked → arrived` is **NOT legal** (confirmation first). Appointment Detail derives its actions from `legalTransitionsFrom()` (APPT.P2) — **the Day-Board does NOT**: `DayBoardActionController` accepts `arrive,start,complete,cancel,no_show` for any status and the server refuses. Not a breach (the server is authoritative either way) but **the same product answers "what may I do?" two different ways on two screens**, and Arrive-on-booked is live and refused. Classified backend/UI gap S4. **FENCE 5 — SOFT-SUSPEND.** Online booking is refused when `! ($branch->active && $branch->accepts_online_bookings)`; **staff booking is deliberately unaffected** — correctly-more-real, and asserted against the seeded suspended branch. `active` and `bookable_online` are separate gates and both are honoured by the finder and the public form. **FENCE 6 — WAITLIST.** Ceiling APPROVE, acceptance through the locked path, declining costs the patient nothing (the entry returns to `waiting` — proven by the fixture). **THE BLOCKER CONFIRMED: `WaitlistService::create()` has NO route and NO UI** — nothing can enter the waitlist in production except a seeder, so the whole feature is unreachable; screens 2 and 4 both need it and it is the first thing any waitlist gate must fix. The mock's audit names (`scheduling.waitlist_booked`, `waitlist.offer_declined`) are invented — the real ones are `waitlist_offer.offered/.declined/.expired`. **ALSO NOT REAL (verified):** a PRICE or tariff code on `Service` (Billing owns price); slot **granularity** as a setting (hardcoded 30-minute stride in the finder); **min notice online**; per-PROVIDER buffers (buffers are per-SERVICE); waitlist preferred days/times/earliest date; SMS/phone consent channels; a **booking-confirmation** notification (only `appointment.reminder` exists — so Public Booking's "A confirmation is on its way" asserts an email nobody sends, D-179); an ICS "add to calendar". **BETTER BACKED THAN EXPECTED:** `ResourceAvailability` already carries BOTH the weekly template (`weekday`+start/end) AND dated exceptions (`date`+`is_available`+`reason`), so Provider Availability is largely a UI over an existing model — and its "saving won't move or cancel them / the server never auto-cancels" copy is EXACTLY the real posture. **PROPOSED CHAIN: 4 core + 1 optional** — SCHED.P2 service catalog+create (cheapest, unblocks a clear story) · P1 Day-Board parity incl. S4 · P3 availability (largest honest win, model already there) · P4 waitlist create+list (needs the `priority` decision first) · optional P5 booking confirmation. **DECLINES (D-188):** screen 8 **No-Show Follow-Up** as drawn — its organising idea is "triage by clinical risk", which nothing records and which the fence centrally prohibits; a plain no-show WORKLIST is buildable and should be specified as its own screen rather than shipped as a reduction. Also declined: the conflict-resolver RANKING (detecting/showing a conflict is right; scoring the fix is the optimiser), waitlist auto-send, and clinical priority. B4 (`Resource` capability) stays deferred — none of these 8 need it; it is owned by the Dental chair gate and APPT.P4. Docs: `docs/wireframe-parity/SCHEDULING-BATCH-DIFF.md` (8 screens, the real-machinery map, the does-not-exist table, per-screen deltas, all six fence areas, shared components + gaps S1–S7 and B4, the proposed chain). **Nothing fixed, no app code, no tests, no seeders; `resources/prototype/` stays gitignored.**
- `a5b3e8d` SCHED.P2 (first of the SCHEDULING chain): Scheduling — **the service catalog: list, editor and archive.** P0D.GU. **Opening:** top commit `7390006` (the scheduling audit), tree clean, CI `completed / success` from the check-run API. **REDIS PROBED AND NOT REACHABLE** (not installed). **The audit's `<pending>` LOG hash backfilled to `7390006`** as this gate's carried-forward housekeeping. **WHAT ALREADY EXISTED AND WAS NOT REBUILT: `ServiceCatalog` was already a complete service-layer write path** — create/update/delete in transactions, with real validation (name/code non-empty, duration > 0, buffers >= 0, at least one resource type), a PER-TENANT UNIQUE CODE, and branch links guarded against a cross-tenant branch id. The audit's S3 was a UI gap, not a backend one. **This gate wrote NO new domain rule** — the controller validates request SHAPE and delegates. **One real defect fixed at the edge:** the catalog's `InvalidArgumentException` (duplicate code) would have surfaced as a **500**; it is now converted to a validation error, and a cross-tenant branch id to a 404 — WITHOUT restating any rule, so the service stays the single authority. **THE SCREEN STATES WHAT THE ENGINE READS**, because a service is not a label — four fields are read at booking time: `default_duration_minutes` (the length of every generated slot, `AvailableSlotFinder`) · `buffer_before/after_minutes` (widen the no-double-book window, in `assertNoOverlap`'s SQL) · `requires_resource_types` (`assertResourceTypesMatch`) · `active` (gates the day-board quick-book list AND public exposure) · `bookable_online` (public exposure only). An admin shortening a duration is editing the booking engine and is told so in words. **ONE SOURCE, PROVEN NOT ASSERTED:** a test edits a duration THROUGH THE SCREEN and then asks the FINDER for slots — the generated slot length moves 30 → 90. The mutation making the finder ignore `default_duration_minutes` turns it red. **The slot stride became `AvailableSlotFinder::SLOT_STRIDE_MINUTES`**, a public constant the page displays, so the number an admin reads is literally the one the scan uses instead of a `30` retyped in a template. **ARCHIVE, NEVER DELETE — AND THE GUARD IS THE DATABASE.** `appointments.service_id` carries **`ON DELETE RESTRICT`**: a referenced service cannot be removed at all. Right guarantee (a booked visit must keep knowing what it was for), wrong presentation (a raw driver error). **So the screen offers NO delete**; `active = false` is the soft state that stops NEW use while existing appointments keep their service — the BRANCH.P1 shape. Both halves tested: an UNREFERENCED service deletes cleanly (the positive control, so the refusal is about the reference, not about deletion being broken) and the referenced one throws while its appointment survives; the route list is asserted to contain **no destroy route at all**. **THE OMISSIONS, RENDERED:** no price/tariff (money lives in the tenant-authored Billing tariff catalog and the issued invoice is the legal figure — a price here would be a second source), no slot-granularity setting, no min-notice-online, no per-provider buffers, no suggested/typical duration. The money test asserts no money-shaped COLUMN on `services` and no money-shaped KEY in the payload **with a POSITIVE CONTROL that pricing genuinely exists in Billing** (a real `TariffItem.unit_price_minor`) — otherwise the assertion would also pass in a product with no pricing at all, which is a different and wrong claim (D-184 shape). **D-191 — THE ORDERING COLUMN THAT ISN'T:** verified against the live schema that `services` has NO `sort`/`order`/`rank`/`position`/`priority` column; `ServiceCatalog::list()` orders by `name`. So the UI writes no ordering field and this gate deliberately added none. D-191 remains a live constraint for SCHED.P4 (the waitlist), not for this screen. **Tests added:** `tests/Feature/Scheduling/ServiceCatalogScreenTest.php` (10 tests, ~1507 assertions). **MUTATION-CHECKED TWELVE WAYS.** Nine caught first time: a price field in the payload · the permission dropped to `appointment.manage` · the update path un-scoped from its tenant · the controller writing the model directly instead of through the service · the stride replaced by a duplicated literal · the usage count faked · the omission loop emptied · the price omission no longer naming Billing · the finder ignoring the service duration. **THREE ESCAPED, EACH A DEFECT IN THE TEST RATHER THAN THE CODE.** (1) **Hardcoded counts stayed green — D-189 FOR THE FOURTH TIME:** the fixture's counts were exactly `3/2/1`, which is what the hardcoded triple said; the test now adds a service and asserts the counts MOVE to `4/3/2`. (2) **A granularity field added to the form stayed green:** the scan looked for the template binding `form.granularity`, which a field added to the reactive object never produces — each declined concept is now COUNTED and may appear only where the omission list names it. (3) **Deleting `ServiceCatalog`'s duration rule stayed green:** the controller's own `min:1` request rule answered first, so the service's guard was never the deciding factor — the same guard-behind-a-guard shape as GOV.P5's free-text opt-in and PT.P7's tenant binding; the service rule is now pinned by CALLING THE SERVICE DIRECTLY (D-183) with a positive control. **STATED, NOT SILENTLY ACCEPTED: service-catalog writes are NOT audited.** Nothing in `Modules\Scheduling` writes an audit row directly — the module is architecturally barred from `Modules\Audit\Models`, and appointment transitions reach the trail by dispatching `AppointmentTransitioned` for an app-layer listener. Adding an event + listener for service changes is the in-pattern fix, but it is a design decision deserving its own gate rather than being smuggled into a UI parity gate. Flagged, not built: a tenant-configuration change that alters what patients can book currently leaves no trail. **A PROCESS NOTE:** a study workflow was launched to fan out the code reading; 4 of its 8 agents died on a session limit and it returned no synthesis. The study had already been done directly, so nothing was lost — but the finding is that the parallel read added no value over reading the six files myself. **VERIFY:** `npm run build` green · `vue-tsc` clean for `Admin/ServiceCatalog.vue` · Pint passed · PHPStan L5 "No errors" · the whole `tests/Feature/Scheduling` suite green (113 passed, 1 skipped) · full `composer check` green · `composer test:smoke` green. **No existing behaviour test modified** (`ServiceCatalogTest.php`'s 6 tests untouched and green). Docs: `SCHEDULING-BATCH-DIFF.md` §16 + the SCHED.P2 chain row and S3 marked DONE.
- `09f6c5b` SCHED.P1 (second of the SCHEDULING chain): Scheduling — **day-board parity: the board now answers "what may I do?" the way the server does.** P0D.GU. **Opening:** top commit `a5b3e8d` (SCHED.P2), tree clean, CI `completed / success` from the check-run API. **REDIS PROBED AND NOT REACHABLE** (not installed). **SCHED.P2's `<pending>` LOG hash backfilled to `a5b3e8d`.** **ONE ANSWER, NOT TWO.** New `AppointmentService::boardActionsFor($status)` — the ONLY domain addition — maps the board's five verbs onto the statuses they move to and asks `legalTransitionsFrom()`, the accessor Appointment Detail already used (APPT.P2). **THE D-156 COMPOSE IS PRESERVED WITHOUT BEING SPECIAL-CASED:** `arrive` on a `booked` appointment is offered because the method asks whether BOTH edges (`booked→confirmed`, `confirmed→arrived`) are legal — if either were ever removed from LEGAL_TRANSITIONS the compose would stop being offered on its own. Result over the fixture: booked → arrive/cancel/no_show · confirmed → arrive/cancel/no_show · arrived → start/cancel (**no arrive**) · in_progress → complete · completed/cancelled/no_show → **nothing**. **AND A CORRECTION TO MY OWN AUDIT.** The scheduling batch diff claimed "the `booked → arrived` case is live: Arrive on a booked appointment is offered and refused". **THAT WAS WRONG** — `DayBoardActionController` COMPOSES confirm→arrive, so that action always succeeded. The real refusals were the others the board offered unconditionally: `start`/`complete` on a booked appointment, `arrive` on an already-arrived one, and ANY action on a terminal one. The finding stood; the example was mis-chosen. Corrected in the diff doc in place. **NO OFFERED ACTION CAN BE REFUSED — PROVEN BY DRIVING THEM:** the test walks every (status, action) pair the board actually offers, posts it through the real transition route and asserts the appointment MOVED; a separate test forges an action the board does NOT offer and confirms the service still refuses it (the list grants nothing — the machine stays authoritative). **NO OVERRIDE.** Every book still runs `lockResource` → `assertNoOverlap`. The test books into an occupied slot through the board's own quick-book route and asserts the refusal, **with a positive control that the identical booking succeeds at a free hour** (so the refusal is the guard, not a broken fixture), then repeats the conflicting request carrying `override`/`keep_both`/`force`, asserts it is STILL refused, and asserts `BookingService` reads none of those parameters. `withoutExceptionHandling()` was needed — over HTTP the framework turns the refusal into a 500 page and the assertion would have passed for the wrong reason. **THE CARVE-OUTS, KEPT PLAIN:** utilisation = booked minutes over the branch's own scan window, two recorded quantities and a division; the idle lane is present at 0, not hidden; no tint/band/rank, and the lane order is asserted to be the RESOURCE order. Waiting = elapsed minutes since the recorded `checked_in_at`, with `null` (not zero) where nobody checked in, **no threshold anywhere** — the test asserts the component never COMPARES the elapsed value and that the line binds no class to it. **HONEST WAITLIST COPY:** behaviour unchanged (APPROVE ceiling, acceptance through `BookingService`), but the audit's over-claim is not repeated — the board now says an outstanding offer holds the patient's PLACE IN THE QUEUE, NOT THE SLOT, and a test asserts both the new wording and that `assertNoOverlap` never consults `waitlist_offers`. **Tests added:** `tests/Feature/Scheduling/DayBoardParityTest.php` (9 tests, ~4859 assertions). **MUTATION-CHECKED TWELVE WAYS.** **A TWELFTH MUTATION FOUND A REAL TEST GAP:** making the grid's `offers()` return TRUE unconditionally left every assertion green, because they all checked the PAYLOAD and none checked that the COMPONENT consults it — Pest cannot execute a template, so the test now reads the component and asserts `offers()` answers from the list and that all five buttons (exactly five) are gated by it. Caught: the board offering every verb · the compose dropped · terminal statuses offering cancel · the overlap guard removed · a tint band on utilisation · the idle lane hidden · `checked_in_at` faked · the waiting line tinted · the grid ignoring the server list · the omission loop emptied · the waitlist over-claim restored. **ONE WAS A NO-OP, RECORDED RATHER THAN COUNTED (D-187):** removing the `whereNotIn([cancelled, rescheduled])` from the utilisation query changes nothing, because `transition()` DELETES an appointment's resource links on both statuses and the sum is per LINK — proven from the code, not guessed. The filter is kept deliberately (it states the intent independently of that implementation detail) and the comment now says so. **THREE TEST DEFECTS FOUND BEFORE THE CODE WAS CLEARED**, all previously-seen shapes: a scan forbidding `amber` and `noShowRisk`, which appear ONLY in the comment and omission list that decline them (**third occurrence** — COMMS.P2 and SCHED.P2 hit it too, now scanned by occurrence count); a `text-danger` scan that matched the pre-existing Cancel button (narrowed to the waiting line itself); and an expectation of 240 booked minutes where 210 is honest, because a cancelled appointment releases its resource links. **A FIXTURE NOTE:** the app clock runs a day behind this shell, so the fixture date is chosen BY QUERY (first weekday with no appointments) rather than by arithmetic — three failed attempts (weekend with no availability, a day already occupied, a service needing a room) preceded the working one. **TWO DEFECTS ONLY THE BROWSER COULD FIND, both invisible to a green suite.** (1) **THE OMISSION CARD RENDERED NOWHERE** — it had landed INSIDE the quick-book slide-over (`v-if="quickBookOpen"`), so it existed only while that dialog was open. The test asserted the iterated keys and the `t()` call were present, per GOV.P3, and they were: **source presence is not reachability.** Moved into the main column. (2) **THE WAITING TIME WAS WRONG TWICE, FOR TWO DIFFERENT REASONS** — it showed `0 min`, then `152 min`, for a check-in 34 minutes old. A naive `Y-m-d H:i:s` is ambiguous both ways: the VIEWER's machine was UTC−7 and parses a naive string as browser-local (so a recent check-in read as the FUTURE and clamped to zero), and `ApplyTenantLocaleTimezone` sets PHP's default zone to the practice's (Europe/Zurich) while `config('app.timezone')` stays UTC — so **Eloquent RE-LABELS the UTC-stored string as Zurich** and shifts it two hours. That middleware's own docblock already flags per-widget datetime→timezone display conversion as an open follow-up. The fix reads the RAW column, parses it as UTC (the documented storage contract) and computes the elapsed **on the server**, so neither ambiguity reaches the screen. D-091's family. **Carried forward: any other widget rendering an absolute time from a naive timestamp is subject to the same relabel.** **VERIFY:** `npm run build` green · `vue-tsc` — the one DayBoard error is PRE-EXISTING (asserted by stashing: same error, line 120→125) · Pint passed · PHPStan L5 "No errors" · full `composer check` green · `composer test:smoke` green. **No existing behaviour test modified.** Docs: `SCHEDULING-BATCH-DIFF.md` §17 + the SCHED.P1 chain row and S4 marked DONE + the §4.4 correction.
- `<pending>` SCHED.P3 (third and FINAL core gate of the SCHEDULING chain — **the SCHEDULING batch core, and with it the BUILDABLE PARITY PROGRAMME, is now COMPLETE**): Scheduling — **provider / resource availability.** P0D.GU. **Opening:** top commit `09f6c5b` (SCHED.P1), tree clean. **CI ON `09f6c5b` WAS STILL `in_progress` WHEN THE GATE ARRIVED** — the gate made green a stop condition, so I did the housekeeping and the READ-ONLY study, held the build, and started only once the check-run came back `completed / success`. **REDIS PROBED AND NOT REACHABLE** (not installed). **SCHED.P1's `<pending>` LOG hash backfilled to `09f6c5b`.** **THE MODEL AND ITS PRECEDENCE (the part most likely to be restated loosely, so the page states it in words):** `ResourceAvailability` = resource · weekday · start/end · date · is_available · reason, in two shapes the MODEL validates in its own `booted()` hooks (recurring = weekday 0-6 + timed window; dated = a date plus either a timed window or, for an unavailability, no times at all). `AvailabilityService::windowsFor()` combines them: **(1) a dated AVAILABLE row REPLACES the weekly template for that date — it does not add to it; (2) dated UNAVAILABLE rows are SUBTRACTED from whatever base remains; (3) a FULL-DAY block empties the day entirely.** **ONE SOURCE, PROVEN BY EFFECT:** the page's effective hours ARE `windowsFor()` — the same method `AvailableSlotFinder` calls at its line 150 and `BookingService::assertWithinAvailability()` uses. The test withdraws a Friday morning THROUGH THE PAGE and then asks the FINDER: every remaining slot starts at or after 13:00, there are fewer of them, and the page agrees because it is the same reader. Mutations to the precedence rules and to the page computing its own windows all turn it red. **TWO THINGS THAT DID NOT EXIST.** (1) **There was NO write path** — `ResourceAvailability` rows were created ONLY BY SEEDERS; `AvailabilityService` reads and nothing wrote (the same shape as the waitlist-create blocker). So `AvailabilityWriter` is new and deliberately THIN: the model already owns the shape rules and they are not restated; the controller converts its `InvalidArgumentException` into a validation error rather than a 500 (the SCHED.P2 pattern). (2) **Availability writes are NOT audited** — no audit action exists, the same finding SCHED.P2 recorded for the service catalog. Flagged, not built. **THE CONSEQUENCE — AN UNGUARDED GAP, STATED AND PINNED (the operationally important finding).** **Withdrawing availability under a booked appointment is not blocked, warned about, or recorded anywhere.** `assertWithinAvailability()` runs at BOOKING time ONLY; nothing re-checks when availability later changes, so the appointment simply ends up outside its resource's hours, still booked, with nobody told. **I did NOT invent a guard** — refusing edits the rest of the system permits would diverge from what booking actually enforces, and a control implying protection it has not got is the D-176 failure. Instead: the page COUNTS what a withdrawal would sit over and says so before saving ("Saving will NOT move, cancel or flag them… they stay exactly where they are, outside these hours, and nobody is told"); the omission card names the missing guard as a refusal; and **a test PINS THE GAP** — it drives the withdrawal, asserts the appointment is untouched, and asserts the resource genuinely has no hours left that day, so if a future gate adds a guard the test fails and forces the decision to be explicit rather than arriving by accident. **THE VIEW:** weekly templates (including a resource with NONE, shown honestly — a mutation hiding it turns the suite red), dated exceptions of both kinds with their **RECORDED REASONS printed verbatim** (never parsed, categorised or tinted), and the effective hours. Plain asymmetric counts (D-166/D-174). **BRANCH.P1 STATED, NOT IMPLIED:** a soft-suspended branch still has availability and staff still book into it — `accepts_online_bookings` gates the public form alone; asserted in both directions. **Tests added:** `tests/Feature/Scheduling/AvailabilityParityTest.php` (9 tests, ~1405 assertions) — **all nine green first time**. **MUTATION-CHECKED THIRTEEN WAYS:** the page computing its own hours · a dated available row adding instead of replacing · a full-day block no longer emptying the day · the exception reason dropped · the empty template hidden · counts hardcoded · the controller writing the row directly · the impact count faked as zero · the permission dropped · the update path un-scoped · the soft-suspend flag inverted · the omission loop emptied · the unguarded-withdrawal warning softened into a false promise. **A PROCESS MISTAKE, CAUGHT AND WORTH RECORDING:** I re-ran one mutation MANUALLY while a background BATCH of mutations was still in flight. Both harnesses snapshot-and-restore the same files, so one restore clobbered the other and **mutation 7's edit was left in the working tree** — the controller was writing the model directly instead of going through `AvailabilityWriter`, and the suite was green because that mutation's own test had already run. I caught it by scanning EVERY touched file for residue rather than trusting the harness to have cleaned up, restored the line, re-ran, and then ran the remaining mutations strictly SEQUENTIALLY with a residue check appended. **Same class as the shared-DB collision (GOV.P5): never run two things that mutate the same state at once — and after any mutation run, verify the tree is pristine before believing a green suite.** **A SMALL INACCURACY FOUND IN PASSING:** `ResourceAvailability`'s docblock declares `@property string|null $date`, but its `date:Y-m-d` cast yields a Carbon at runtime — PHPStan caught the mismatch. The controller parses explicitly (as `AvailabilityService` already does) and the comment NAMES the discrepancy rather than relying on it; correcting the docblock would touch every reader of that property and is a small follow-up. PHPStan also needed two STRUCTURAL fixes rather than suppressions (a filtered Eloquent collection loses its row type — partitioned with a typed loop instead). **VERIFY:** `npm run build` green · `vue-tsc` clean for `Availability.vue` · Pint passed · PHPStan L5 "No errors" · full `composer check` green · `composer test:smoke` green. **No existing behaviour test modified; `AvailabilityService` untouched, so the finder's behaviour is unchanged.** Docs: `SCHEDULING-BATCH-DIFF.md` §18 + the SCHED.P3 chain row and S2 marked DONE + **the batch core marked COMPLETE with P4 DEFERRED by decision (unreachable feature; keeps D-191 closed by absence) and No-Show Follow-Up DECLINED under D-188**.
